# Resonant interfaces (frozen contracts)

Later phases build against these contracts. Changing one requires updating this file, `architecture.md`, and every implementation in the same PR. Code is the source of truth; file paths are given for each contract.

## Event: `resonant_sdk.events.Event`

```python
class Event(BaseModel, extra="forbid", frozen=True):
    id: str            # ULID (resonant_sdk.new_id), time-sortable
    source: str        # "imessage" | "slack" | "webhook" | "scheduler" | "ext:<name>" | "claude" | "gate"
    type: str          # "message" | "job.due" | "approval.reply" | "human_input" | "ext.<name>.<event>" ...
    payload: dict[str, Any] = {}
    task_id: str | None = None      # None: unrouted, goes to the router
    principal: str | None = None    # always set by core from an authenticated identity
    dedupe_key: str | None = None   # UNIQUE: duplicates are dropped on ingest
    occurred_at: datetime
    trace_id: str
```

- Events are append-only. The only change ever made to one is setting `consumed_at`.
- `Loop.submit(event) -> bool` returns False for a duplicate. An event with a `task_id` wakes that task if it is waiting.
- Unrouted events go to the router. If the router raises, the event is kept and retried each tick, and after 5 failures it is dead-lettered with an audit row. If the router returns None, the event is consumed. If it returns a `NewTask`, the task is created and the event becomes its first input.
- An extension can never set `principal`; core overwrites it.

## Task envelope: `tasks` table, `resonant.loop.types.Task`

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `kind`, `runner` | free text; `runner` selects the `Runner` (`local`, `reminder`, `job`, `claude`, `cu`, `echo`) |
| `extension`, `principal`, `lane` | scope and requester; `lane="oncall"` gets reserved capacity |
| `priority` | `PRIORITY_ONCALL=0 < PRIORITY_OWNER=1 < PRIORITY_MEMBER=2 < PRIORITY_BACKGROUND=3`; set by core from lane and principal, never by the model |
| `status` | `queued`, `running`, `waiting`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled` |
| `wait_reason` | `approval`, `human_reply`, `usage_reset`, `ci`, `veto`, `resource` (deferred: a worker slot, repo lock or turn is busy); a CHECK constraint requires it exactly when status is `waiting` |
| `wake_at` | for `waiting`, when to wake; for `queued`, not before (retry backoff, or a reminder's due time). `NewTask.wake_at` (default None) sets it at creation |
| `attempts`, `step_no` | `attempts` counts failures and crashes and resets on progress; `step_no` counts persisted steps |
| `checkpoint` | opaque JSON owned by the runner, written every step |
| `claude_session_id`, `worktree_path`, `scope` | runner state that must survive a crash |
| `origin_event_id`, `channel_ref`, `trace_id` | provenance, where to reply, and tracing |
| `title`, `title_outside` | PER-279, migration 025: a short human line for the dashboard (`Look up net worth`), set once by `insert_task`: `NewTask.title`, else `store/task_titles.derive` in code (no model call): an owner's text, its cleaned first six words from the origin event (`Photo or file` for media with none); a job `<ext>/<job>`; a watch `Watch W3: <what>`; a run its ask's first eight words (`Merge R4: …` for a merge watch); an ask the owner's own words, else the question; a reminder `Reminder: <text>`; a reply or command its code-built title; a refused approval or batch reply `Approval reply: <reason>` (never the typed code), a worker reply `Reply to R4`. Redacted (`redact.clip_text`, 80 chars) before it is stored. `title_outside` is 1 when the words came from outside text (a tainted task's question or ask). Existing rows are backfilled by 025's Python step (`task_titles.backfill`, run by `store.db.migrate` in the same transaction) |

## Runner: `resonant.loop.types`

```python
class Runner(Protocol):
    name: str
    uses_claude_slot: bool
    max_step_s: float | None          # overrides LoopConfig.stuck_after_s
    async def step(self, task: Task, events: list[Event], ctx: StepContext) -> StepOutcome: ...

class StepContext(Protocol):          # call from the event-loop thread only
    def save(self, *, checkpoint=None, claude_session_id=None, worktree_path=None) -> bool: ...

StepOutcome = Continue(checkpoint)                                  # requeue now
            | Wait(reason, checkpoint, wake_at=None)                # park; frees a Claude slot
            | Done(result, checkpoint=None)
            | Fail(error, retryable=False, checkpoint=None, retry_at=None)
```

Loop guarantees:
1. Each step's outcome, checkpoint, and consumed events commit in one transaction.
2. An event that arrives during a step that returns `Wait` requeues the task, so no wakeup is lost.
3. A retryable `Fail` keeps its events for the retry. Backoff is 5s·2ⁿ up to 300s, or `retry_at` if set.
4. A crash mid-step means the task resumes from its last checkpoint with its events replayed, and the crash counts as an attempt. A graceful shutdown requeues without using an attempt.
5. A step that exceeds `max_step_s` is cancelled and treated as a retryable failure.
6. An outcome that isn't JSON-serializable becomes a non-retryable failure. A database error while persisting stops the loop: the daemon exits, launchd restarts it, and `resume()` recovers the task.
7. **Next wake.** Between ticks `Loop.run` sleeps `Loop.wait_s()`: `tick_s`, or less when the earliest future `wake_at` of a queued or waiting task (`store.tasks.next_wake_at(conn, after=last_tick)`) comes sooner; one that fell due since the last tick means tick at once. A task is dispatched within loop precision of its `wake_at`, not up to a tick late. Tasks already due at the last tick are excluded, so they never cause a busy loop. With `Loop.scheduler` set, its `next_due(after=last_tick)` counts too.
8. **Scheduler.** `Loop(..., scheduler: TickHook | None = None)`; `TickHook` is `async tick(now)` plus `next_due(after) -> datetime | None`. Each tick runs `scheduler.tick(now)` after routing and before waking and dispatching, so a job task it creates is dispatched on the same tick. A scheduler error is logged and never stops the loop.

Runner obligations:
- Make steps idempotent relative to the last checkpoint. Side effects go only through the executor.
- On `CancelledError`, clean up any subprocess, then re-raise.
- Save anything a retry must not lose (session ids, worktrees) with `ctx.save` as soon as it exists.

**Claude slots** (`SlotPool`): a cap (`claude.max_concurrent`, default 5) with `reserved_oncall_slots` (default 1) that only on-call can use. Slots are taken in dispatch order (priority, then age) and held only while a step is in flight. Running sessions are never preempted.

## ToolSpec: `resonant_sdk.tool`

```python
class ToolSpec(BaseModel, frozen=True, extra="forbid"):
    name: str               # ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$
    description: str
    args_schema: dict       # JSON Schema
    effect: Effect          # read | write | pay
    level: Level | None     # L0..L3; None means DEFAULT_LEVEL[effect]: read=L0, write=L2 (GT-1), pay=L1
    source: str             # "builtin" | "ext:<name>"
    extension: str | None
    timeout_s: float = 30
    secrets: tuple[str, ...]
    commit: bool = False    # send/pay/delete: always pauses inside a computer-use scope
    untrusted: bool = False # BR-2: the result is outside content (files, logs, transcripts); a loop taints the task (brain-plan D6)
    taint_exempt: bool = False  # PER-245: runs on its level even in a tainted task (ToolCore gates it as inferred); builtin L2/L3 writes only
    grant: str | None = None  # BR-2: ^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$; a member may request it with grants {"builtin:<grant>": [request]}
    approval_summary: str | None = None  # PER-268: "merge PR #{number} in {repo}"; see below
    approval_detail: str | None = None  # PER-300: one string arg shown in full under the prompt; see below
    worker_requestable: bool = False  # PER-296: a worker may ask for it with `resonant request`; extension tools only
    effective_level: Level  # property
```

`approval_summary` (PER-268) is the tool's own phrase for approval prompts, failure texts and dry runs, filled from the call's args (`gate.summary.fill_summary`), lower case, as what the tool would do: `{arg}` is the arg's value (`clean_external`'d, clipped to 40 with `...`), `[...]` a part shown only when every arg in it is set (a boolean prints nothing: it is the condition), e.g. `start a run[ on {repo}]: "{ask}"[{merge} (merges if clean)]`; `{~arg}` names an arg left out on purpose (`merge PR #{number} in {repo}{~head_sha}`). Every other set arg follows the phrase in parentheses (`describe_args`: `create a GitHub repo named garden (private=false)`), so a template never hides what a call does. `resonant_sdk.tool.parse_summary` checks it (one printable line of at most 120 characters, `[ ]` not nested, braces only as `{arg_name}`). A missing arg outside `[...]`, or a list or object value, falls back to the arg list.

`approval_detail` (PER-300) names one declared **string** arg the approval prompt shows in full, for what a 40-character value can't carry (a SQL statement, a request body). `gate.summary.detail_text(intent)` builds the block from the call's args: the value in a code fence, line breaks kept, tabs as spaces, a ```` ``` ```` inside shown as `'''`, approval-code look-alikes masked with `mask_codes` (so a `yes K4` inside can't answer another prompt), at most `DETAIL_MAX` (4096) characters, not normalised (what runs is what is shown); a mask or a cut adds `(code-like text shown as ?; cut at 4096 characters; /approvals shows it unchanged)`. Unset, blank or not configured: None. The summary line still names that arg (clipped), so a batch prompt, which lists lines only, shows it as for any tool. `ToolSpec` refuses an `approval_detail` that isn't a string arg of its schema. The gate sets it on `NeedsApproval.detail` (both a new ask and the pending one asked again; never stored on the approval row: `/approvals <code>` shows the args), `approval_prompt` copies it to `ApprovalPrompt.detail`, and `ApprovalPrompt.text()` appends it on its own lines after the question. Batch prompts carry summaries only. `ApprovalPrompt.text()` turns a ```` ``` ```` in the line itself into `'''` when a detail follows, so the fences stay balanced. Slack splits a prompt longer than a section (`blocks.split_sections`: at line ends, at most 3000 characters and 20 sections, never inside an `&amp;`-style entity, a fence open at a split closed and reopened), for the buttons message and its resolved edit alike.

These invariants are enforced at registration:
- `pay` is always L1.
- `read` without secrets is always L0.
- A `commit` tool must have a side effect.
- Only a builtin (`extension=None`) L0 read without secrets can carry a `grant`.

The model supplies only the tool name and args. Effect and level always come from the registry.

## Manifest: `resonant_sdk.manifest.Manifest` (`resonant.yaml`, `version: 1`)

```yaml
name: <slug>                     # required; not "core", not a builtin command word
version: 1                       # required
description: <text>              # optional, <= 120 chars: shown to humans (`ext list`); no model reads it since PER-256
examples: [<line>, ...]          # optional, <= 3 lines of <= 80 chars: router few-shots
uses: <first-party ext>          # optional; allows exactly one extra block named after it
approvers: [<principal>, ...]    # each must hold the approve grant for this extension
jobs:     [{id, schedule (5-field cron), tz?, critical?, run,
            effect? (default write), level?, secrets?, timeout_s? (<= 1800, default 300),
            catchup? (none | duration <= 24h, default none)}]
tools:    {<name>: {run, description?, effect, level?, args: {<arg>: string|integer|number|boolean},
                    secrets?, commit?, timeout_s? (<= 300), approval_summary?,
                    approval_detail? (PER-300, a declared string arg),
                    worker_requestable? (PER-296, default false)}}
          # {placeholders} in run and approval_summary must be declared args
mcp:      {servers: {<id>: {transport: stdio, command, args?: [<literal>, ...],
                             tools: [{name, description?, effect, level?, commit?, secrets?, timeout_s? (<= 300),
                                      approval_summary?, worker_requestable?}]}
                       | {transport: http, url: https://…, auth?: bearer, secret?: <declared secret>,
                             tools: [{name, description?, effect, level?, commit?, timeout_s? (<= 300)}]}}}
          # id ^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$; only listed MCP tools are ever exposed
notify:   {on: [<event>, ...], channel: imessage|slack}   # "item" is reserved (pulse reports): refused here
report:   {max_urgency: urgent|normal|low}   # optional; the highest urgency report() may use (no section: reports refused)
health:   {check, every: 30s|5m|1h}
secrets:  [<name>, ...]          # ^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}$; every tool, job and panel secret must be declared here
panels:   [{id (slug, unique), title (1-60), description? (<= 160), command: [<argv>, ...] (1-16 printable-ASCII
            items of <= 200, no { or }), refresh: {idle: <numeric 5-field cron>, active?: <same>}, tz?,
            secrets?, timeout_s? (<= 120, default 30), max_bytes? (1024-32768, default 16384)}]   # <= 8; PER-280
```

- Unknown keys are an error. A tool name defined in both `tools` and an MCP server's `tools`, or in two servers, is an error. `McpServer.command` is one printable word (arguments go in `args`, printable ASCII, at most 32 of at most 300 chars); `McpStdioServer.secrets` is the union of its tools' secrets. `McpHttpServer.url` passes `mcp_url_problem` (https with a host, no user info or fragment; `http://` only for a loopback host with a port, which the daemon refuses outside tests: `checks.mcp_url_error`, `checks.ALLOW_LOOPBACK_HTTP = False`); `auth: bearer` and `secret` come together; its tools declare no secrets, and `McpHttpServer.secrets` is `(secret,)` or `()`. Every server's secrets must be declared at the top level. `McpServer` is the union, discriminated by `transport`.
- Load manifests with `load_manifest` / `parse_yaml`. In that loader only `true`/`false` are booleans, so `on:` stays a string key.
- `tool_specs()` returns the tools (CLI and MCP). Each CLI `args_schema` has `additionalProperties: false`. An MCP spec carries the manifest's description, effect, level, secrets, commit and timeout, and a placeholder schema: the host replaces it with the stripped schema pinned at install (`ExtensionRecord.mcp_pins`).
- `job_specs()` returns one `ToolSpec` per job: `name=f"job_{id}"` with dashes as underscores, `description="scheduled job"`, args `{due_at: string}` (required, closed), and the job's effect, level, secrets and timeout. Job specs never enter a model toolset or a member's commands. A tool name equal to a job spec name is an error.
- `panel_specs()` returns one `ToolSpec` per panel (PER-280): `name=f"panel_{id}"` (dashes as underscores), `description="dashboard panel"`, `effect=read`, level L0, args `{due_at: string}` (required, closed), the panel's timeout and secrets. Panel specs never enter a model toolset. `Panel.run` is `shlex.join(command)`, the run string every command check takes (`checks.commands()` lists it as `panel <id>`, so `argv0_error`, `dir_errors` and the network escalation apply). A panel spec name that equals a tool or job spec name is an error, panel ids must be unique, and a panel's secrets must be declared at top level. Its crons are five numeric fields in range (`cron_values`, no names or `@`); `PANEL_MIN_INTERVAL_S` (60) is the floor. The output contract is `resonant_sdk.panel_schema` (`docs/panel.schema.json`); the stdlib-only builders are `resonant_sdk.panel`. See `docs/extensions.md` §6a.
- **Cadence.** A job fires no more often than every 5 minutes, and at most hourly when its effective level asks (L1/L2). `cron_min_interval_s(schedule)` is computed from the minute and hour fields, assuming consecutive days both fire. `Job.catchup_s` is the catch-up window in seconds (0 for `none`).
- `resonant.extensions.checks`:
  - `approver_errors(manifest, principals)` validates the approvers against this installation: each must be a listed principal holding `approve` for the extension (any of its tools), never a workspace-default member (PER-293).
  - `grant_warnings(manifest, principals)` (PER-293): per-tool grants (a principal's or the default's) naming a tool the extension doesn't define; `ext validate` prints them as warnings (they grant nothing).
  - `name_errors(manifest)` refuses a name equal to a builtin command word (`/help`, `/ext`, …).
  - `dir_errors(root, manifest)` checks the extension directory: **stdlib only** (no `requirements*.txt`, lock files, `package.json`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `Pipfile*`, `environment.yml`, or `pyproject.toml` declaring or dynamic dependencies, at any depth), **no symlink resolving outside the root**, the **argv[0] allowlist** for every tool, job and health command, and `mcp_command_error` for every MCP server.
  - `argv0_error(root, run)`: argv[0] must be a `.py` file inside the root (`./cli.py`; in-dir files run only as Python under the daemon interpreter), `python3 <.py file inside the root>`, `gh` or `git`. Fetch-runners (`npx`, `bunx`, `pnpx`, `pipx`, `uvx`, `uv`, `docker`, `podman`, `curl`, `wget`), shells (`sh`, `bash`, `zsh`, `dash`, `fish`, `env`), placeholders and anything else are refused.
  - `mcp_command_error(root, command)`: an MCP server's `command` is a `.py` file inside the root (`./server.py`, run as `[python, -I, -S, <script>, *args]`), or a binary by name or absolute path. Fetch-runners and shells (as above) and interpreters and package managers (`node`, `deno`, `bun`, any `python*`, `ruby`, `perl`, `npm`, `pip`, `brew`, …) are refused by name. `resolve_mcp_command(root, command, path=EXT_PATH)` returns the path to pin: the in-dir script, or the binary's real path, which (like `resolve_bin`) must be unwritable all the way up or a guarded Homebrew keg. Pinned under `argv0_paths["mcp:<id>"]` (`mcp_key`).
  - `resolve_argv0(root, run, path=EXT_PATH)` returns the absolute path to pin: the file, `sys.executable` for `python3`, or `resolve_bin` for `gh`/`git`.
  - `resolve_bin(name, path=EXT_PATH)` looks `gh`/`git` up on `/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin` and returns its real path, or raises `ValueError` when it is missing or a Claude worker could replace it: the real file and every dir above it must be unwritable by this user, or `sandbox.guarded_tool(found)` must hold (a `DAEMON_TOOLS` link in an install dir into its own formula's keg under the same Homebrew prefix, which `default_protected` write-protects). A manifest's `bins` are pinned with it under `argv0_paths["bin:<name>"]` (`bin_key`) and passed as `RESONANT_BIN_<NAME>` (`bin_env`).
- **trade-jev boundary:** order execution never goes through the loop or gate. Core only sees trade-jev's jobs, read tools, events, and kill switch. Its own process owns execution and its own risk controls.

## Principals: `resonant.principals` (`~/.resonant/principals.yaml`)

```yaml
principals:
  justin:    {role: owner, identities: ["imessage:+15551234567", "slack:U01"]}
  brian:     {role: approver, identities: ["slack:U02"]}                              # PER-293
  cofounder: {identities: ["slack:U03"], grants: {dori: [request, approve]}}           # whole extension
  miu:       {identities: ["slack:U04"], grants: {dori: {request: [file_ticket, find_tickets]}}}  # per tool
  family:    {identities: ["imessage:+15557654321"], grants: {"builtin:files": [request]}}   # BR-2
default: {channel: slack, grants: {dori: {request: [file_ticket, find_tickets, staging_status]}}}  # PER-293
```

- An identity is `imessage:<E.164 | lowercase email>` or `slack:<user id>`. An email handle must start with a letter or digit (never `-`, which osascript could read as an option).
- There is exactly one owner, and an identity can belong to only one principal. A principal's name may not contain `:` (that shape names a workspace-default member).
- **Roles** (PER-293): `owner | approver | member` (default `member`).
  - The owner can do everything, including `admin`: `/update`, `/ext …`, `/pulse`, `resonant secret`, editing principals, and the kill switch's fan-out to sibling instances.
  - An approver may `request` and `approve` everything: every builtin (global scope) and every extension. Never `admin`, and never an `OWNER_TOOLS` builtin from any path: the `ADMIN_TOOLS` (`update_resonant`, `mute_extension`, `list_extensions`, `pulse_preview`, `shell`, `shell_read`) and, since PER-318, the `TRUST_TOOLS` (below). `can()` refuses them, so the brain never offers them and the gate asks only the owner (`approvers(None, tool)` is `(owner,)` for them). `Principals.approver_names` lists them in file order; `is_approver(name)` is true for the owner and approvers.
  - A member can do only what their grants list.
- **Grants.** A grants value is `[request, approve]` (the whole extension, as before) or `{request: [tool, …], approve: [tool, …]}` (`ToolGrants`: only those tools). A per-tool grant naming a tool the extension doesn't define grants nothing (`ext validate` warns, `checks.grant_warnings`). A `builtin:` key stays a list of `request` only.
- **Workspace default** (PER-293, optional): `default: {channel: slack, grants: {...}}`. `resolve(identity, *, vouched=False)`: an identity on that channel that no principal claims, and that the adapter vouches for (same Slack `team_id`, PER-295; `e2e.FakeChannel(vouch=True)` in tests), resolves to the synthetic principal named by the identity itself (`slack:U0123`): role member, the default's grants. `get(name)` returns a listed principal or that synthetic one, `is_default(name)` says which. It is never an approver: its grants may not list `approve`, and it can't be named in a manifest's `approvers:` (`checks.approver_errors`). iMessage never gets a default (`channel` is `slack` only).
- `can(principal, action, extension, *, grant=None, tool=None)`:
  - The owner can do everything; an approver everything but `admin`.
  - For a member, `admin` is refused and global scope (`extension=None`) needs a global `request` whose `grant` (the tool's `ToolSpec.grant`) the member holds as `"builtin:<grant>": [request]` (BR-2). A `builtin:` grant may list only `request`.
  - **Claude** (PER-293): `ask_claude` carries `grant: claude`. A member holds it with `"builtin:claude": [request]`, or on a **team config** (`Principals.team`: an approver or a workspace default) with any extension `request` grant (Claude is how they use those tools), and Claude then offers them only what `may_request` allows (`BrainRunner.tools_for`, `wiring.tool_access`). Any other member still gets `MEMBER_REPLY`, as before.
  - Otherwise a member can do only what their grants list; a per-tool grant needs `tool` (without it, it covers nothing).
- `holds(principal, action, extension)`: whether they hold `action` on any of the extension's tools (approver checks, approver listing without a tool).
- `may_request(principal, spec)` is `can(principal, "request", spec.extension, grant=spec.grant, tool=spec.name)`: what the gate, the dry-run gate, the router's toolsets and the command list check.
- The local CLI acts as the owner (`owner:cli`).
- **Trust rules** (PER-294, PER-318): stay instance-wide, and only the owner changes them. Approvers approve one action at a time; they don't teach. No one but the owner requests or approves `add_trust_rule`, `allow_trust_rule`, `remove_trust_rule` or `allow_tools`, whatever their role or grants (`principals.TRUST_TOOLS`, part of `OWNER_TOOLS`): the gate denies the call (`not permitted to request this tool`, audited `gate.evaluate`), nothing changes, and `/rules forget …` / `/rules allow …` from an approver gets `not allowed`, audited `router.command_refused` (an approver still lists rules with `/rules`). Only the owner's prompt offers `yes always` (`NeedsApproval.offer_to`); anyone else's `yes always` on an approval that offers it is a plain yes: it approves this one action, saves no rule, and the reply says so in one line: `Approved this once. Only <Owner> can make it standing.` (`router.approvals.once_reply`, the owner's display name). Owner-only Resonant is unchanged.
- **Commands** (PER-293): an owner-only command is `admin` unless the registry marks it `staff` (`Command.staff`): `/run`, `/runs`, `/cancel`, `/merge`, `/repos`, `/repo`, `/rules`, `/approvals`, `/restart`, `/disk`, `/logs`, `/kill`, `/resume`, which approvers may use too (`commands.allowed`). `/update`, `/ext` and `/pulse` stay the owner's. On a team config, a member's `/run` or `/merge` gets `start_work_reply` (else `not allowed`, as before): "Only Justin or Brian can start work." (the owner, then the approvers) plus " I can file a ticket for it instead." when they may request a `file_ticket` tool. An `/<ext>` command needs `request` on the tool it names (any of its tools for a usage line, and to be listed).
- **The instance-only pause** (PER-293): an approver's `/kill` (or the plain word `pause`, approvers only) engages this instance's kill switch with `paused_by = approver:<name>` and never fans out to siblings; their `/resume` (or `resume`) releases only an approver's pause: a pause personal Resonant made (`paused_by = sibling:resonant`) gets `SIBLING_PAUSE_REPLY` ("Justin paused everything from personal Resonant; he resumes it there."), any other (the owner's, or no source) `OWNER_PAUSE_REPLY` ("Justin paused this; he resumes it."), audited `router.resume_refused`. Any non-approver engaging over an approver's pause takes `paused_by` over (`killswitch.engage`). Members can do neither.

## Tool registry: `resonant.tools.ToolRegistry`

`register(spec)`, `get(extension, name)`, `specs(extension=None)`. Specs are keyed by `(extension, name)`, and builtins use `extension=None`, so two extensions may each define a tool with the same name. The registry is the only source of a tool's effect and level.

## Gate: `resonant.gate`

```python
Intent.for_tool(spec: ToolSpec, *, task_id, principal, args, scope_id=None, trace_id=None,
                origin: Origin = "inferred")
    # effect / level / commit / tool / extension are read from spec: callers can't set them
    # args are validated with canonical_json and deep-copied
    .hash  # recomputed on every access:
           # sha256(canonical_json({tool, extension, args, effect, level, commit}))
           # origin is not hashed: an approval binds to what runs, not to who asked
Origin = Literal["explicit", "inferred", "scheduled", "tainted"]   # anything else raises ValueError
canonical_json(value)  # sorted keys; rejects non-str keys, NaN/Infinity, non-JSON types

Decision = Allow(token, approval_id=None, unattended=False)
         | Deny(reason)
         | NeedsApproval(approval_id, code, approvers, expires_at, summary, effect, level, require_code)
         | Veto(approval_id, until, summary)
         | DryRun(would)

class Gate(TokenVerifier, Protocol):
    def evaluate(self, intent: Intent) -> Decision: ...
    def consume(self, token: str, intent_hash: str) -> bool: ...   # single-use; executor calls it
    def pending_for(self, principal: str) -> Sequence[PendingApproval]: ...
    def open_batch(self, principal: str, approval_ids: Sequence[str]) -> ApprovalBatch: ...  # BA-1
    def resolve(self, resolution: ApprovalResolution) -> ResolveResult: ...   # by id: button / CLI
    def resolve_reply(self, reply: ApprovalReply) -> ResolveResult: ...      # text reply: gate binds it
# PolicyGate only (BA-1, toolcore.BatchGate): batch_max; owner; approval_states(ids) -> {id: status}
#   (after lazy expiry); drop(ids, reason=) expires pending ones (audited approval.expired)
```

**Origin** says who chose the action. Code sets `"explicit"` in exactly one place, the local runner's `direct_call` path, whose checkpoint only the router's deterministic command parser writes. Everything the model chooses is `"inferred"`. A job that fired on its schedule is `"scheduled"`: only the scheduler and the job runner set it, and the gate treats it like `"inferred"`, so it is **never explicit**. The router and the local runner never set it (a test scans them). A call the model chose in a task that has read untrusted content (files, logs, transcripts, the web, email, extension results, `ask_claude` output; docs/brain-plan.md D6) is `"tainted"`: the gate asks for L2 **and L3**, an L0 read still runs, an L1 still needs its code, and no standing allow applies (they need `inferred`), so text the task read can't get a write through without the owner. On an instance whose owner set `gate.tainted_writes: allow` (PER-335, a self-sufficient team instance; default `ask`, which changes nothing) the owner's and an approver's tainted L2/L3 writes run as an untainted L3 would: see **Tainted writes** below. The one exception is a `taint_exempt` spec (`add_note`, `add_reminder`, `remember`, `forget`; PER-245): ToolCore (`gated_origin`) sends its tainted call to the gate as `inferred`, so it runs on its level's rule. The gate itself never exempts anything. Only runner code that tracks taint sets it (the brain loop, BR-1); the approval summary is code-built either way. Origin is never read from model output.

Evaluation order (Phase 2, `resonant.gate.PolicyGate`):
1. **Registration:** if `registry.get(extension, tool) != intent.spec`, Deny. An intent can't claim an effect or level its tool wasn't registered with.
2. **Permission:** if `can(principal, "request", extension)` is false, Deny.
3. **Unsafe args:** if `unsafe_args(args)` finds anything, Deny. The reason gives only a count, never the keys or values. Then the tool's `refusals` hook, if it has one: a reason means Deny with it, never an ask (the shell family's never-list, PER-258).
4. **dry_run:** a tool that is neither an L0 read nor store-internal becomes `DryRun(would=<summary>)`. The summary uses the tool's code-built heading when it has one (`restart Ollama`), as approval prompts do.
5. **Level × origin × autonomy** (`policy.rule`):

| Level | autonomy on, explicit | autonomy on, inferred / scheduled | autonomy on, tainted | autonomy off |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L0 | Allow | Allow | Allow | Allow |
| L3 | Allow | Allow | NeedsApproval | NeedsApproval |
| L2 | Allow | NeedsApproval | NeedsApproval | NeedsApproval |
| L1 (destructive, pay) | NeedsApproval, `require_code=True` | same | same | same |

A `levels` choice (`merge_run` picking L3) doesn't change a tainted call's answer: it still asks. The one change to the tainted column is `gate.tainted_writes: allow` (PER-335, below): L3 and L2 writes of the owner and approvers become Allow (L2 as L3); L0 and L1 rows are unchanged, and so is the autonomy-off column.

**Tainted writes (PER-335, `tainted_writes=`, config `gate.tainted_writes: ask | allow`, owner-only config file, default `ask`).** `ask` is today's behavior, byte for byte: the gate never reaches the check, and its audit rows carry no new key. With `allow`, in step 5 a `tainted` call is weighed as `inferred` at L3 (so it runs, unattended) when **all** of these hold, else it asks as before: autonomy on (the kill switch still asks for everything; dry run is step 4 and still wins); the principal is the owner or a `role: approver` (a member's or the workspace default's tainted call asks as before, and its grants still decide what it may request at all, step 2); the registered effect is `write` (never `pay`, never a read) at L2 or L3, and still L2 or L3 after `levels`; the tool is an extension's, or a builtin named in `gate.policy.TAINT_LIFTABLE_BUILTINS` (an allowlist: `start_run`, `cancel_run`, `merge_run`, `answer_worker`, `message_worker`; a test pins every builtin L2/L3 write). So `OWNER_TOOLS` (admin, the brain shell, trust) never lifts, nor does anything that would outlive the turn untainted (`add_job`, `create_watch` and the other job and watch tools: a stored prompt later fires as `scheduled`), `add_ci`/`merge_ci_pr` (a workflow onto the default branch with no flagged-paths check), the repo set (`create_repo`, `add_repo`, `forget_repo`), restarts, or deleting notes and reminders; and `Standing.tainted_allows(intent)` (`StandingAllows`): the call has a rule scope (a tool `NEVER` stands for, a shell verb or a repo that can't be named as a scope never qualifies), its `stands` hook passes (`merge_run`: `merge_standable`, the same bar as a `yes always` merge: no flagged paths, a push-mode repo, no `ask merge` rule), and no `ask` trust rule decides it (the standing allows' precedence, above). A gate built without `standing` never lifts. L1 and pay always ask with the code from the configured approvers, whatever the origin or setting. A lifted call is `Allow(unattended=True)` (so `merge_run` merges strictly: green CI, CLEAN, `--match-head-commit`), counts toward the task's `MAX_SIDE_EFFECTS` (past it, it asks with no `yes always`), posts its status line in the thread like any L3 (PER-319, PER-323), and its `gate.evaluate` row carries `tainted_allowed: true` when it ran (not when it then asked: capped, or a rejected intent). With the owner's direct ask (PER-272) the lift comes first: a lifted merge runs as a plain unattended L3 (no `owner_asked` approval row). The trade-off the owner takes: text the instance reads could steer it into any extension's L2/L3 write (including one that posts what the turn saw) or a run (a worker that works like the owner at the keyboard; `message_worker` and `answer_worker` also steer a run already going), never an L1 one.

`evaluate` never returns `Veto` yet (veto windows are deferred); the type stays for them. Reporting after an L3 or explicit Allow (`Done: <summary>`) is the runner's job.

**Standing allows (GT-1, `standing=`, `resonant.trust.tools.StandingAllows`).** Between steps 4 and 5, an owner's own `inferred` call of a tool at L2 (after `levels`) runs as L3 when the owner's trust rules hold an `allow` rule for that tool: `action = "tool:<name>"` (builtin) or `"tool:<extension>.<name>"`, on its natural scope (the call's `repo` argument, the registered name when the registry knows it; else the extension; else `*` for a builtin). Matching is the worker rules' precedence over the `ask` rules and the `allow` rules for exactly this tool, so a broader worker `allow` (`allow * in *`) never widens a tool and an owner's `ask` rule at least as specific still asks. Never for any other principal, never for `explicit` (already Allow), `scheduled` or `tainted` calls (a tainted call's prompt offers no `yes always` either; `gate.tainted_writes: allow` lifts tainted writes on its own terms, above, and reuses these rules only to let an `ask` rule or a `stands` hook keep a call asking), never for L1 or pay, never with autonomy off (the kill switch asks for everything) and never in dry run (step 4 comes first). `allow_trust_rule`, `allow_tools` and `remove_trust_rule` never take one (loosening stays the owner's own word per change). `merge_run` can since PER-272 ("stop asking before merging run PRs, just merge when green"), but only where its `stands` hook says so (`StandingAllows(conn, stands=…)`, `tools.runs.merge_standable`: a run with no flagged paths in a push-mode repo no `ask merge` rule covers). The first ask for such a call offers it: the approval gets `allow_always` and `always_scope`/`always_action` (migration 013), its summary ends `(yes always = don't ask before running github.merge_pr in jhe/dori)` and the prompt `Reply yes / yes always / no`. The owner's `yes always` saves exactly that rule (`created_from="yes always"`, audited `trust.rule_added` with `approved_by`) in the reply's transaction; the call runs, spending the approval, and the report adds `Rule added: … (rule N)`. `/rules` lists the rule and `/rules forget <n>` removes it. The `gate.evaluate` audit row carries `standing` (true when a standing allow made the call L3, false when one was checked and none matched).

```python
PolicyGate(conn, principals, registry, *, dry_run: bool, approval_ttl_s: float,
           actions: {(extension, tool): (Intent) -> str | None} = {},
           approvers_for: ApproversFor = no_manifest_approvers,
           levels: {(extension, tool): LevelFor} = {},
           standing: Standing | None = None, clock=utcnow, batch_max=10,
           refusals: {(extension, tool): RefusalFor} = {},     # PER-258
           summaries: {(extension, tool): SummaryAction} = {},  # PER-258: the whole line
           owner_asks: OwnerAskCheck | None = None,             # PER-272
           tainted_writes: "ask" | "allow" = "ask")             # PER-335; anything else: ask
RefusalFor = Callable[[Intent], str | None]     # why a call is never allowed (Deny, no ask)
Standing  # Protocol: allows(intent) -> rule | None; offer(intent) -> StandingScope | None;
          # tainted_allows(intent) -> bool (PER-335)
ApproversFor = Callable[[str], Sequence[str]]   # extension -> the names its manifest lists
LevelFor = Callable[[Intent], Level | None]     # per-call level; may only pick L2 or L3 for a
                                                # tool registered at one of them (merge_level)
PolicyGate.approvers(extension: str | None, tool: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, ...]
rule(level, origin, autonomy_on) -> "allow" | "ask" | "ask_code"
autonomy_on(conn) -> bool
NOT_APPROVED, APPROVAL_EXPIRED, APPROVAL_USED, NO_TASK   # Deny reasons the runner templates
REPLY_CHANNELS = KNOWN_CHANNELS  # {"imessage", "slack"} (PER-292); the identity must still be the actor's
STORE_INTERNAL_TOOLS, is_store_internal(spec)             # resonant.tools
```

- **Kill switch:** kv `autonomy` is read on every `evaluate`. Anything but `"on"` (missing, garbage, unparseable) counts as off: fail closed.
- **dry_run exemption** is keyed on registry identity: `is_store_internal(spec)` is true only for a builtin (`extension=None`, `source="builtin"`, no secrets) named in `STORE_INTERNAL_TOOLS = {add_note, delete_note, clear_notes, add_reminder, cancel_reminder}`. A test pins the set. An extension tool with the same name is not exempt, and ops tools never are. Exempt tools still go through step 5.
- **Approvers** (`approvers(extension, tool=None)`, PER-293). A builtin (`extension=None`, global scope): the owner, then every `role: approver` in file order; no manifest is consulted (owner-only config: `(owner,)`, as before). An extension tool's approvers are those, then each name from `approvers_for(extension)` (the manifest's `approvers`, untrusted) that holds `approve` for that extension in `principals.yaml` (for `tool`, under a per-tool grant; the gate passes the intent's tool), in manifest order, deduped. A name without the grant, an unknown name or a workspace-default member is dropped and logged, so an extension can never grant itself approvers. Self-approval is allowed (an approver answers the prompt for their own request). Batches, `yes always` (PER-318: an approver's is a plain yes), standing allows and the owner's direct ask stay the owner's. The default `no_manifest_approvers` returns `()` (owner only) until the extension host supplies manifests. A pending approval keeps the approvers it was created with; `resolve` and `resolve_reply` re-check the grant (for the approval's tool) at resolution time, so an approver removed while a prompt waits is refused and the owner can still answer.
- **Approvals** per (task, intent hash). "Ask" reuses the pending approval if there is one; otherwise it inserts one with `approvers=gate.approvers(extension)`, `expires_at = now + gate.approval_ttl_s`, a fresh code, and a code-built summary (`summarize(intent, action=actions[(extension, tool)](intent))` when an action is registered). An intent whose `task_id` has no task row (the router's fast path) gets `Deny(NO_TASK)`.
  - Approved and unused: `Allow(token, approval_id)`.
  - Any rejected approval for the intent: `Deny(NOT_APPROVED)`, even if the table would now Allow (the owner's "no" holds for that intent).
  - Expired: `Deny(APPROVAL_EXPIRED)`. Used: `Deny(APPROVAL_USED)`; a used approval never yields another Allow.
  - So an intent gets at most one approval per task: after it expires, is rejected or is used, the gate never re-asks. A retry needs a new task. (Different intents of one task each get their own approval; since BA-1 a task asks about several at once as a **batch**, below.)
  - An Allow by the table (L3, explicit, or a standing allow) still spends an approved, unused approval for the same intent, if there is one (the `yes always` that just made the call L3).
- **Lazy expiry:** `evaluate`, `pending_for` and `resolve` first run `expire_due(now)`. The waiting task's `wake_at = expires_at` is the backstop that wakes it.
- `resolve(resolution)` (CLI, button): the actor must be in `approvers` and hold `approve`; then `store.approvals.resolve` and `wake_resolved` in one transaction, audited `approval.resolve`. Refusal reasons: `not_found` (also for a non-approver, so ids aren't confirmed; the audit row says `not an approver`) or the store's reason. A refused attempt is audited `decision="refused"`. This authenticated path approves L1 without typing the code.
- `resolve_reply(reply)` (text replies) binds the reply to one approval or one batch (see **Binding** below), then `store.approvals.resolve(..., event_id=reply.event_id)` (for a batch: each of its approvals in one transaction, one refused rolls back the rest; `yes 1,3` approves those and rejects the others, PER-255) and `wake_resolved` in one transaction. Every attempt writes one `approval.resolve` audit row with every reply field (raw text, event id, `occurred_at`, code, path, channel, actor identity, comments), the reason, and for a batch `batch_id` and `approval_ids` (for `yes 1,3`, PER-255: `picks`, `approved_ids` and `rejected_ids`, so each approval's decision is on record).
- `pending_for(principal)` lists unexpired pending approvals as `PendingApproval`, a batch as **one** entry: its first approval's id and code, the summary `N changes: <line>; <line>…` (200 chars), its strictest level and effect, `require_code` if any approval needs it, the earliest expiry, `changes` (its size; 1 for a lone approval, PER-255). A batch asked of someone else is left out.
- **Batches (BA-1).** `open_batch(principal, approval_ids)` asks the owner about several pending approvals **of one task** as one prompt: it stores the snapshot (`approval_batches`, migration 002: `id, principal, approval_ids` in order, `created_at`, `used_at` when answered), sets every approval's `expires_at` to `now + approval_ttl_s` (they expire together) and audits `approval.batch` (batch id, ids, code, level, `require_code`). The same set again returns the batch already open, unchanged (a re-prompt). `ValueError` for fewer than 2 or more than `batch_max` (`gate.batch_max`, 10) approvals, a `principal` who isn't the owner, an approval that isn't pending, another task's, one the owner can't approve, or one already in another batch. `ApprovalBatch(batch_id, principal, approval_ids, created_at, code, task_id, expires_at, lines, require_code, destructive, level, effect)`: `code` is its first approval's, `lines` each approval's summary (`batch_line`: its `yes always` offer cut only as the suffix of an approval that offered one, so model-chosen args never shorten a line; a trust-widening builtin, `allow_tools`, `allow_trust_rule`, `remove_trust_rule`, is prefixed `widens trust: `), `approvers == (principal,)`. **One unit for binding:** only its code names it (its other approvals' codes are never shown and are `unknown_code`), a plain yes/no counts it as one pending item and is judged only among the prompts the actor got (an approval pinned in a turn that hasn't asked yet never makes it ambiguous), it needs `yes <code>` when any approval does (L1, pay: the strictest level) or when one widens trust, `yes always` is `always_unsupported`, and only the owner it was asked of can answer it (a member who approves its extension gets `nothing_pending`, even with the code). `resolve` by id of any approval in an open batch resolves the whole batch (the owner only). **Some of it (PER-255):** `yes 1,3` / `yes 1,3 D7` (`ApprovalReply.picks`, the prompt's numbers) binds like a plain or coded yes, then approves those changes and rejects the rest in the same transaction (the batch is used: nothing of it binds again). The strictest-level rule applies to the picked changes: the code is needed when any of them is L1 or pay or widens trust (`_Unit.needs_code(picks)`), so `yes 1` may skip the code for an L2 change next to an L1 one. Picks for a lone approval are `not_a_batch`; a number that isn't one of the batch's changes is `unknown_pick` (nothing is decided). Text only: `resolve` by id has no picks. A batch whose approvals aren't all pending any more (one decided on its own: a race) can't bind; the runner drops the rest (`drop(reason="batch_split")`).

**The owner's direct ask (PER-272, `owner_asks=`, `resonant.trust.asked.OwnerAsks`).** A call carrying `Intent.asked = OwnerAsk(text, event_id, lookups)` (unhashed, like `origin`; only `ToolCore.call` sets it, from `CallScope.asked`, which only the Claude bridge fills) that the table would ask about runs as an approval instead when all of these hold: the owner's own call (`principal == owner`), registered L2 and `effect: write` (never L1, pay or L3 by registration), origin `inferred`, or `tainted` with `lookups` only `list_runs` and only for `merge_run`; autonomy on, not dry run (step 4), not `capped`, not one the owner already said no to, no approval for the intent yet, and `OwnerAsks.check(intent)` returns None (the text names exactly this change: per-tool matchers for `merge_run`, `allow_tools` and `linear.save_issue` create; no relayed instruction, question, hypothetical or negation; see the module doc). The gate then inserts an approval and resolves it approved in the same transaction (`decided_by: owner_asked`, `approval.resolve` with `decision: owner_asked`, `path: owner_asked`, `event_id`, `lookups`), and returns `Allow(token, approval_id, unattended=True)` that the executor spends like any yes (strict: the owner saw no prompt with this call's details). A second owner-asked change of the same tool from the same message event asks. For `merge_run` the matcher also asks for a run with flagged paths or in a repo an `ask merge` rule covers. Anything else asks as before. The `gate.evaluate` row carries `owner_asked`: `owner_asked` when it ran so, else the short reason it didn't (`ambiguous`, `relayed`, `tainted`, `title not named`, ...).

**Unattended (PER-272).** `Allow.unattended` is true when no prompt showing this call was answered: the gate allows a change with no approval for the intent and not `explicit` (an L3 by level, a standing allow, a trust rule, including a chat `merge_run` that `merge_level` made L3), or on the owner's direct ask (also after a crash, from the approval's `decided_by`). The executor passes it as `CallContext.unattended`; `merge_run`'s handler then merges strictly (`ClaudeRunner.merge_run(code, unattended=True)`: green CI, no flagged paths, CLEAN `mergeStateStatus` (`Workspace.merge(require_clean=True)`, UNKNOWN read again up to 3 times), `--match-head-commit`; reported by the chat reply's report line, and a merge that waited for CI by its own text whatever the digest level).

**Allow tokens.** Every `Allow` carries a single-use token that the gate issued for one intent hash. After an approval, the runner re-evaluates the intent on wake and gets an `Allow(token, approval_id)`. Tokens live in memory, so after a restart the runner simply re-evaluates.

Every evaluation writes one `gate.evaluate` audit row (decision, origin, autonomy, dry_run, approval id, deny reason, args). `DryRunGate` (Phase 0, kept as a test double) checks registration and permissions, allows L0 reads with a token, and returns `DryRun(would=<decision name>)` for everything else. It has no approvals.

**Transactions.** `transaction()` is not reentrant. Helpers that write bookkeeping (audit, spans, gate, executor, kill switch) use `atomic()`, which joins an enclosing transaction via SAVEPOINT. So they are safe to call standalone or inside a caller's transaction.

Kill switch (`resonant.killswitch`; the owner, and since PER-293 an approver on its own instance: `paused_by = approver:<name>`, see Principals): `engage` sets `autonomy="off"` and `paused=true`; `release` reverses both. Both write an audit row that includes the prior state. Migration 002 writes explicit default rows. `add_pause_hook(fn) -> remove` registers `fn(conn, paused)`, called after `engage` (`True`) or `release` (`False`) commits; a hook that raises is logged and never undoes the switch. The scheduler uses it to kill extension processes on `/kill` at once; a CLI `resonant kill` (another process) is seen on the scheduler's next tick, which then runs every pause hook itself (`fire_hooks`, PER-290: runs cancelled, the brain session stopped, as for an in-process `/kill`). PER-290: kv `paused_by` is the engaging actor (`paused_by(conn)`); an engage while already paused keeps the first source; `release` deletes it. `SIBLING_SOURCE = "sibling:resonant"`.

Critical-job LaunchAgents run `resonant job-guard --job <ext>/<id> -- <cmd>`. It **fails closed**:
- It never creates or migrates the store.
- It opens it with `open_existing`.
- It exits 75 if the store is missing or its schema is behind, if the `paused` row is missing or isn't a boolean, or if paused is true.
- Before exec it audits `job.exec`. If exec fails, it audits `job.exec_failed` and exits 127.

On-call autonomy checks `paused` before acting.

## Approvals: binding, codes, and replies

Request (`resonant.gate.codes`):
- `new_code(pending)` returns 2 characters, a letter then a digit, with no look-alikes, unique among pending approvals.
- `approval_text(code, summary, *, require_code=False, destructive=False, always=False, phrased=False)`. `phrased` (PER-268: the summary is the tool's own `approval_summary`, `approvals.phrased`, migration 019, carried on `NeedsApproval` and `ApprovalPrompt`; the stored summary stays the bare phrase) reads as a question with the code after it: `OK to merge PR #121 in justinhe16/resonant? [P3] yes / no`; L1 `…? This can't be undone. [D7] yes D7 / no`; pay `…? [P3] reply exactly: yes P3 / no P3`; a standing-allow offer `…? [K4] yes / yes always / no`. Otherwise the forms below:
  - L2/L3: `"[A7] <summary>? Reply yes / no"`.
  - A worker's trust check (`always=True`, EM-4; never with the other two): `"[K4] R4 wants to deploy on dori-api: \"deploy to prod\" (new kind of action; yes always = don't ask before deploy in dori-api). Reply yes / yes always / no"`.
  - L1 (`destructive=True`): `"[D7] <summary>? This can't be undone. Reply yes D7 / no"`. It takes precedence over `require_code`.
  - Pay (`require_code=True`): `"[P3] <summary>? Reply exactly: yes P3 / no P3"`.
- **Plain words first** (PER-324): `approval_text(..., plain=None)`. `plain` is the brain's stated reason for the call, cleaned (`gate.summary.plain_line`): the question in the phrased form without `OK to`, and the exact code-built summary on the next line as inline code (`gate.codes.exact_line`: `` `…` ``, a backtick inside shown as `'`), e.g. ``"Look up the shop's locations (read only)? [A7] yes / no\n`call shop GET /v2/locations`"``; L1 `<plain>? This can't be undone. [D7] yes D7 / no` then the exact line; pay `<plain>? [P3] reply exactly: yes P3 / no P3`; an offer `<plain>? [K4] yes / yes always / no`. A gated `approval_detail` (PER-300) still follows in full, under both lines. No reason (every caller but the Claude bridge, and a call Claude gave none): `plain` is None and the prompt is exactly as before. The plain line is the model's words, so it never replaces the summary: what runs is always shown, code-built, right under it. `plain_line(text)`: `clean_external` (one line, printable ASCII, no brackets, approval-code look-alikes masked), Slack mrkdwn characters (`` ` * _ ~ | ``) dropped and links shown as `(link)`, trailing `. ? ! ; : ,` dropped, first letter capitalised, at most `PLAIN_MAX` (140) characters (a cut ends in `...`); None when nothing readable is left.
- **Where the reason comes from** (PER-324). The Claude bridge (`runners.ask_tools`) adds an optional `approval_reason` string (`maxLength` 300; "one short sentence in plain words for someone who isn't an engineer") to the listed schema of every tool the gate may ask about (`takes_reason(spec)`: anything but an L0 read, and only when the tool has no arg of that name, which then stays the tool's own). `ClaudeTools._call` strips it before the tool's schema check and passes it as `CallScope.reason`; for Claude's own tools (PER-258) it is Claude Code's Bash `description`. `ToolCore.call` pins it cleaned as `PinnedCall.reason` (`checkpoint.call.reason` / each batch item's, only when set; never in `args`, the intent or its hash, so an approval binds the call alone), and `ToolCore` copies it onto the gate's ask (`with_plain`: `NeedsApproval.plain`, for a new ask, a resume and a batch join alike). `approval_prompt` sets `ApprovalPrompt.plain`; a batch prompt gets each change's (`batch_prompt(batch, plains)` / `prompt_batch(..., plains=)`, by approval id, as `ApprovalPrompt.plains` by position). Never stored on the approval row (`/approvals <code>` and the dashboard show the summary), never in a reminder.
- `batch_text(code, lines, *, require_code=False, destructive=False, plains=())` (BA-1; PER-324 `plains`: by position, a change with one reads `N. <plain>` with its exact line under it, indented, as inline code, `ApprovalPrompt.lines` set): `"[D7] OK to make these 4 changes?\n1. linear: save issue id=\"RES-12\", state=\"Done\"\n…\nReply yes / no, or yes 1,3 D7 for just those."`; lines are never clipped (each is the whole code-built summary a lone prompt shows, so a yes covers only what was shown); with an L1 write `Some can't be undone. Reply yes D7 / no, or yes 1,3 D7 for just those.`; with pay (no L1 write) `Reply exactly: yes D7 / no D7, or yes 1,3 D7 for just those.` The pick example is `pick_example(n)`: `1,3`, or `1` for a batch of two (PER-255). `ApprovalPrompt` gains `lines` and `destructive`; a batch prompt's `approval_id` is the batch's id (the channel's dedupe key).
- The summary is built by code from intent fields. Every external string goes through `sanitize_external`, which quotes it, collapses whitespace and newlines, strips code-like tokens, and truncates to 60 characters. `clean_external` does the same without truncating or quoting.
- `resonant.gate.summary`:
  - Order: a hook's whole line (`summaries`), else the tool's action heading (`actions`) with the args, else the tool's `approval_summary` filled (`phrased(intent)`, PER-268), else the arg list.
  - `summarize(intent, *, action=None)` returns the filled `approval_summary` when there is no action and it fills, else `<extension>: <tool name> key="value", …` (underscores become spaces; no extension prefix for builtins), e.g. `add note text="buy milk"`. `action` is an optional code-built heading (`delete all 4 notes`) that replaces the tool name; it is cleaned too and never comes from a model.
  - `describe_args(args)`: sorted keys, at most 3 shown, then `+N more args`. Keys (≤ 20 chars) and values (≤ 40 chars) go through `clean_external`; a clipped value gets `(truncated)`, and a value that lost non-ASCII characters gets `(non-ASCII hidden)`. Numbers, booleans and null print as JSON; lists and objects print as `(list, N items)` / `(object, N keys)`.
  - Masking is lossy on purpose: `"vitamin d3"` shows as `"vitamin ?"`. The full args are available to the owner via `/approvals <code>`.
  - `unsafe_args(args)` returns the JSON paths (`$.text`, `$.items[1]`, `$.<key>`) of keys or strings containing control characters other than tab/newline/CR, format characters (bidi overrides and isolates, zero-width characters, BOM) line/paragraph separators, or lone surrogates. Variation selectors and private-use characters are allowed. The gate denies such intents rather than sanitizing them, so what runs always matches what was shown.
- Config (`gate:`): `approval_ttl_s` (default 1800, 60–86400) is how long a prompt stays answerable; `batch_max` (default 10, 2–20, BA-1) is how many changes wait on one batch prompt.
- **Capped** (PER-261): `evaluate(intent, *, capped=False)`. ToolCore passes `capped=True` for a change past `MAX_SIDE_EFFECTS` in a task that batches: a verdict that would allow it (L3, a standing allow) asks instead (no `yes always` offer); reads and refusals are unchanged; the `gate.evaluate` row carries `capped: true`. The pin keeps it (`PinnedCall.capped`, `checkpoint.call.capped`), so its resume is gated capped too: it runs only on its own approval.

**Binding** (`PolicyGate.resolve_reply`). A reply resolves an approval only if all of these hold:
- It comes from an authenticated identity whose principal is in the approval's `approvers` list and holds `approve` for the extension.
- It arrived on a channel adapter (`channel` is the event's source: `imessage`, not SMS; `slack`, PER-292) via the parser, from an identity that resolves to the actor. The router never builds a reply from a `truncated` payload. Or (PER-295) it is a Slack button click (`path="button"`, `channel="slack"`, `BUTTON_CHANNELS`): a button always carries its prompt's code (`ApprovalReply` refuses a button without one, or with `always`/`picks`), so it binds like `yes <code>`/`no <code>`, `require_code` L1 included; its sent-before rule is that the prompt reached *some* approver before the click (the click is on the posted message, which another approver may see in a shared thread); a pay approval never binds by button (`code_required`).
- **Sent-before.** The approval's prompt was sent to the actor (`Approval.sent_at(actor)`) strictly before the reply's `occurred_at`, with or without a code. A reply never binds a prompt sent after it.
- With a code, it names one of the actor's pending, unexpired approvals (or a batch, by its code). Without a code, exactly one approval (or one batch: BA-1 counts a batch as one) is pending for the actor, both now and among those open and already sent to the actor at `occurred_at`. So an old plain reply re-routed later can't bind what has since become the only pending approval. If it's ambiguous, re-ask and never guess.
- `require_code` (L1, pay): only `yes <code>` approves. A plain `no` may deny under the single-pending rule.
- **After a Claude answer** (PER-261). When a Claude answer went out to the actor after the target's prompt (or its reminder) and before the reply's `occurred_at`, a plain `yes` or `yes 1,3` is `code_required` ("Reply yes A7 to confirm.", or "Reply yes 1,3 D7 to confirm."): Claude's text sits above the reply and can't stand in for the prompt. `store.outbound.claude_since`: the iMessage channel notes each send in send order (a Claude answer before its first chunk goes out, any other text only once it went out, so a failed reminder never counts as shown) (kv `outbound.recent:<principal>`, the newest 32: seq, time, dedupe key, whether its key marks a Claude answer, PER-256: `outbound.is_claude_send(key)`, `ask:` but not `ask:ack:`, since the `Claude: ` prefix is gone; `outbound.note(conn, principal, at, key)`; the last Claude answer also in kv `outbound.claude:<principal>`), and the order decides, so an ack landing after the answer or two sends in one millisecond can't flip it; a prompt that fell out of a full window counts as before the last Claude answer (fails closed). A `no` is unaffected, and the batch prompt that follows a Claude answer (or a reminder) shows the prompt again.
- **By id with `always`** (`resolve(ApprovalResolution(always=True))`, the CLI's `approve --always`): as a `yes always` by text, only for a lone approval that offered it, not `require_code` (else `always_unsupported`); the owner's saves the offered rule in the same transaction, anyone else's is a plain yes (`ResolveResult.once`, PER-318). A `yes 1,3` reply's `approval.resolve` row says `decision="partial"`.
- **Idempotent per event.** If `reply.event_id` already resolved an approval (`resolved_event_id`), the gate returns that original result with no write, wake or audit. A replay under another actor gets `nothing_pending`.
- `yes always` (EM-4, GT-1) binds by the same rules (code or single pending, sent-before), but only to an approval with `allow_always`. Two kinds offer it: a worker's trust check (`trust.checks`; not when an owner's `ask` rule made the check ask, and not when the system is an area of a system already seen or a rule's scope) and an owner's L2 chat tool call that can take a standing allow (see **Standing allows** under Gate). Never `require_code`. For any other approval it is refused as `always_unsupported`, never read as a plain `yes`. The store's conditional UPDATE re-checks it. Only the owner widens trust (PER-318): from anyone else (an approver, a manifest-named approver) a `yes always` on an approval that offers it binds as a plain `yes`: `approvals.always` stays false, no rule is saved, the `approval.resolve` row has `always: true, once: true`, and `ResolveResult.once` makes the router reply `Approved this once. Only <Owner> can make it standing.` The owner's bound `yes always` sets `approvals.always`; for a check the waiting check adds the rule, for a tool approval the gate saves its `always_scope`/`always_action` rule in the same transaction.
- **Worker questions (EM-5).** A plain `yes`/`no` (no code) while a worker question is open for the actor binds no approval when one of the actor's approval prompts is also open: the router replies `Which one? Approval K4 (…), or R4's question. Reply yes K4 / no K4 for the approval, or R4 yes for the question.` With no approval prompt open it goes to the question (`router/relay.py`), not to `Nothing is waiting for approval.` A code (`yes K4`) always binds the approval; `R4 yes` always answers the question. A worker's `trust_check` approval is answerable while its run's task is `queued` (woken, waiting for a Claude slot); other approvals still need `waiting` or `running`.
- `yes with comments: …` approves and appends one `Event(source="gate", type="human_input", payload={approval_id, comments})` to the task (`dedupe_key="approval:<id>:comments"`), before the wake.

Refusals are closed (`ReplyRefusal`, `REPLY_REFUSALS` in `resonant.gate.types`):

| Reason | When |
|---|---|
| `nothing_pending` | Nothing is pending for the actor. A non-approver always gets this, whatever it sends, so pending approvals are never revealed. |
| `ambiguous` | No code and several pending (now or when the reply was sent). Re-ask listing the codes. |
| `unknown_code` | The code matches none of the actor's pending approvals. |
| `code_required` | A plain `yes` for a `require_code` approval. |
| `not_sent_before` | The prompt wasn't sent to the actor before the reply. |
| `unsupported` | Not a channel parser reply, or a `yes all` reply (`all_in_batch`, `batch_id`: not offered). |
| `not_a_batch` | `yes 1,3` for a lone approval (PER-255). Reply: `That one is a single change. Reply yes or no.` |
| `unknown_pick` | `yes 1,3` with a number that isn't one of the batch's changes (PER-255); nothing is decided. Reply: `Not done: D7 has 3 changes. Reply yes 1,3 D7 with numbers from its list.` |
| `always_unsupported` | `yes always` for an approval that doesn't offer it (from anyone; on one that offers it, a non-owner's is a plain yes, PER-318). Reply: `Not done: yes always isn't offered for that one. Reply yes or no.` (or `Reply yes <code> to confirm.` for a coded one). |
| `already_resolved`, `expired`, `task_not_waiting`, `not_found` | The store's conditional UPDATE refused (a race, or the task moved on). |

**Reply parser** (`resonant.gate.replies`, Phase 2 is parser-only):

```python
parse_reply(text, *, truncated=False) -> ParsedReply | None
ParsedReply(decision: approve|deny, code=None, comments=None, always=False,
            picks=None)   # code uppercase; picks: yes 1,3 (PER-255), sorted, distinct
normalize_reply(text) -> str   # NFKC, straight quotes, collapsed whitespace
MAX_COMMENTS_LEN = 500
```

- The whole message must match, after normalization and ignoring (ASCII) case:
  - `(yes|approve|👍)( <code>)?` and `<code> yes`
  - `(no|deny|👎)( <code>)?` and `<code> no`
  - `yes( <code>)? with comments: <text>`, where `text` is 1–500 characters and is kept verbatim
  - `yes always( <code>)?`, `yes <code> always` and `<code> yes always` (`always=True`; plain `yes` only, no emoji or `approve`)
  - `(yes|approve) <picks>( <code>)?` and `(yes|approve) <code> <picks>` (PER-255): `picks` are a batch's change numbers, 1–20, comma separated (`1,3`, `3, 1`); `yes 1 3`, `no 1,3`, `yes always 1,3`, `yes 0` and `yes 77` (a look-alike code) aren't replies
- Outside comments, a trailing run of `.` and `!` is dropped (`Yes.`, `yes!!`, `yes A7.`). `?` never is: `yes?` is a question.
- 👍 and 👎 may carry a skin tone and the emoji presentation selector.
- A code must come from the `new_code` alphabet, so look-alikes (`A1`, `O7`) never match.
- Anything else returns None: `y`, `ok`, `sure`, `yes?`, `yes all`, `yes A7 and C4`, a pasted prompt, or text with invisible characters. A `truncated` payload is never a reply.
- The parser only reads text. Binding (approvers, single pending, `require_code`, sent-before, whether the picks are the batch's numbers) is the gate's job.

**Batches** (BA-1) replace the deferred `yes all` design: a batch is one task's changes asked about together (above, `open_batch`), answered with its own code like any approval; there is no `yes all` across tasks, and `ApprovalReply.all_in_batch` / `batch_id` stay refused (`unsupported`). The snapshot is stored, so a reply approves exactly the approvals in it, never one that arrived later; the audit row records the batch id and each approval it resolved. A subset (`yes 1,3 D7`, [PER-255](https://linear.app/doriapp/issue/PER-255)) approves those changes and drops the rest (rejected); the runner reports each dropped one as `Not done: <summary> (not approved).`

**Deferred** (C4): the approval classifier and `resonant eval approvals`. `ApprovalReply.path` is `"parser"`, or `"button"` for a Slack click (PER-295); `"classifier"` stays refused. With several approvals pending, a plain `yes` gets a re-ask that lists the codes (a batch as one). The design kept for later:
- **Classifier**, only if the parser fails. It is separate from the router and uses a small, focused prompt. Its input is the reply text and the summary, and nothing else. Its output is `{decision: approve|deny|unclear, approval_code, comments, confidence}`. It approves only if `decision == approve` and `confidence >= 0.9`; anything else gets a re-ask.

**Concurrency:** resolution is atomic (`UPDATE approvals … WHERE status = 'pending'`), so the first valid reply on any channel wins. On-call pages (parked) go to iMessage and Slack together with one dedupe key; the other channel's message is updated to "resolved by X via Y".

**Pay:** only a parser match that includes the code (`yes P3`) counts. The classifier is never used for pay, and the same rule applies on Slack.

**Comments:** `yes with comments: …` approves and appends a `human_input` event to the task, which the runner treats as a constraint.

**Gate API for replies.** Channels don't pick approval ids. They pass an `ApprovalReply` and the gate binds it:

```python
ApprovalReply(actor, actor_identity, channel, raw_text, decision: approve|deny,
              path: parser|classifier, event_id, occurred_at, code=None, all_in_batch=False,
              batch_id=None, confidence=None, comments=None)
    # event_id, occurred_at: from the reply Event (C5); occurred_at must be timezone-aware
ApprovalResolution(approval_id, approved, actor, actor_identity, path: button|cli, comments=None,
                   always=False)   # PER-261: `resonant approvals approve --always`
ResolveResult(ok, reason, resolved: tuple[ResolvedApproval(approval_id, task_id, intent_hash, approved), ...], once=False)  # once: PER-318
PendingApproval(approval_id, code, task_id, summary, effect, level, require_code, expires_at)
ApprovalBatch(batch_id, principal, approval_ids, created_at, code, task_id, expires_at, lines,
              require_code, destructive, level, effect)   # stored in approval_batches (BA-1)
```

`ResolveResult.resolved` tells the caller which tasks to wake.

**Store** (`resonant.store.approvals`, migration 003). Plain functions over a connection; the gate owns the policy, this module owns the rows:

```python
NewApproval(task_id, intent_hash, tool, effect, level, summary, approvers, code, expires_at,
            require_code=False, extension=None, requested_by=None)
insert_approval(conn, spec, now) -> str              # IntegrityError if one is pending for (task, intent) or the code is taken
get_approval(conn, id) / pending_for_intent(conn, task_id, intent_hash, now) / pending_by_code(conn, code, now) -> Approval | None
latest_for_intent(conn, task_id, intent_hash) -> Approval | None   # newest, any status
was_rejected(conn, task_id, intent_hash) -> bool
pending_for(conn, principal, now) -> list[Approval]  # unexpired, principal in approvers
open_at(conn, principal, at) -> list[Approval]       # open (created, undecided, unexpired) at `at`, any status now
pending_codes(conn) -> set[str]                      # for new_code
add_channel_ref(conn, id, *, ref, at, principal) -> bool   # keeps the first send per (ref, principal)
resolve(conn, id, *, approved, decided_by, now, event_id=None)
    -> None | "not_found" | "already_resolved" | "expired" | "task_not_waiting"
by_resolved_event(conn, event_id) -> list[Approval]; resolve_result(approvals) -> ResolveResult
approved_unused(conn, task_id, intent_hash) -> Approval | None
mark_used(conn, id, now) -> bool                     # True exactly once
expire_due(conn, now) -> int                         # audits approval.expired
wake_resolved(conn, result, now, *, principal=None) -> int
intent_args(conn, approval) -> dict | None           # full args, from its gate.evaluate audit row
# batches (BA-1, approval_batches): BatchRow(id, principal, approval_ids, created_at, used_at)
insert_batch(conn, principal, approval_ids, now) -> BatchRow; get_batch(conn, id)
batches_of(conn, approval_ids) -> {approval id: BatchRow}   # an approval is in at most one
mark_batch_used(conn, id, now) -> bool; set_expiry(conn, approval_ids, at)   # pending ones only
# one request per action (PER-269, migration 022): Approval/NewApproval.action_key, .action_target
pending_for_action(conn, action_key, now) -> list[Approval]   # oldest first
reply_code(conn, approval) -> str | None             # its code, or its batch's
obsolete_for_target(conn, target, now) -> list[str]  # expire_one(reason="obsolete"); skips batch members
approved_for_action(conn, action_key, *, tool) -> Approval | None
```

- **One request per action** (PER-269, `resonant.gate.actions`). An action Resonant can be asked for by several paths has one identity, `Action(system, verb, target).key`: `github merge jhe/dori#2`. `for_intent(conn, intent)` names `github.merge_pr` (merge) and `github.rerun_failed` (rerun) from their `repo`/`number` args, and `merge_run` from the run's registered repo and PR; `for_check(conn, run, action, what)` names a worker check with `--action merge` (the PR of the run's own repo named in `what`: `owner/name#N`, `#N`, `PR N`, else the run's own PR; another repo's PR gives none). The approval stores the key and target. Before `PolicyGate._ask` opens an approval it looks for a pending one with the same key (any task, any tool): if there is one, the call is `Deny("R8 is already asking the owner for this, so no second prompt went out; …")` (`already_asking`; it carries no approval code, as no tool result does), and no second prompt goes out. The same task asking again for the same tool with other args (`merge_pr` at a newer `head_sha`) supersedes its own stale ask (`expire_one(reason="superseded")`) unless that one is in a batch. `TrustChecks` shares the run's own open check for the same key (reworded), and answers `handled` (exit 1, "don't do it yourself") when another path holds it.
- **Obsolete** (PER-269). When a PR is merged or closed, the pending approvals targeting it expire with reason `obsolete` (audited `approval.expired`, nothing texted; reminders and prompts list pending ones only): after a successful `github.merge_pr` (`ToolCore`), a `merge_run` merge (`ClaudeRunner._merge`), a run's PR found `MERGED`/`CLOSED` (`_collect`), and the github watch's `pr_merged`/`pr_closed` events (`scheduler.persist_events`). An approval in an open batch is left to its batch.
- **Resonant's own merge** (PER-269). `merge_record(conn, slug, number)`: a succeeded `github.merge_pr` for that PR (its `executor.result` and `gate.evaluate` audit rows) is `MergeRecord("resonant", code)`; an approved check of that run for it is `MergeRecord("run", code)`. `_collect` ends a run whose PR is `MERGED` with such a record `done` (`set_merged`, `set_merged_by`, audit `run.done_merged` with `by`, `approval`): "R8's PR #2 is merged into main (you approved it in M6)." Only a merge with no record keeps the ⚠ alert and fails the run.

- `resolve` is one conditional UPDATE: the row must be pending, `expires_at > now`, and its task `waiting` or `running`. It records `decided_by`, `decided_at` and `resolved_event_id`. The refusal reason is read afterwards and is informational.
- **Single use.** `used_at` is set once, with the executor's `started` row. Once approved, an approval has no expiry; it is valid for the life of its task.
- **Orphans.** Every `store.tasks` path that makes a task terminal (`set_status`, `save_step`, `requeue_interrupted`) expires its pending approvals in the same transaction and audits `approval.orphaned`. Decided approvals are left alone.
- **Waking.** `wake_resolved` inserts `Event(source="gate", type="approval.reply", task_id, payload={approval_id, approved})` with `dedupe_key="approval:<id>:resolved"`, and requeues the task only if it is `waiting`. A running task sees the event when its step returns `Wait`. Call it in the resolver's transaction.
- `Approval.sent_at(principal)` is the first time the prompt went to that principal (from `channel_refs`, entries `{ref, at, principal}`), for the sent-before rule.

**Audit.** Every field of the reply goes to the audit log: the raw text, the path, the confidence, the actor identity, the code, and the batch id. Approvals carry `expires_at`. There is at most one pending approval per (task, intent hash), and pending codes are unique.

## Executor: `resonant.executor`

```python
idempotency_key(task_id, step_no, call_index, intent_hash) -> str
Executor(conn, verifier: TokenVerifier,
         handlers: {(extension | None, tool): async (args) -> dict | ContextHandler},
         *, secrets: SecretStore | None = None, result_max_bytes: int | None = None)
    .execute(intent, decision, *, step_no, call_index=0) -> ToolResult
    .recorded(key) -> ToolResult | None   # what executions holds for key; read-only
    .resolve_ambiguous(key, *, succeeded, actor, result=None, note=None) -> bool   # audited
ToolResult(ok, data, error, duration_ms, replayed, ambiguous, idempotency_key,
           events=(), notice=None, reason=None)   # reason: PER-268, below
stderr_reason(tail, extension=None) -> str | None   # the last meaningful stderr line, cleaned
class ToolFailed(Exception)   # raise from a handler: "definitely did not take effect"
CallContext(secrets: {RESONANT_SECRET_<NAME>: value}, idempotency_key, task_id, events: list,
            origin="inferred", unattended=False)   # PER-272: Allow.unattended
class ContextHandler(ABC): async __call__(args, ctx: CallContext) -> dict
secret_notice(SecretError) -> str
```

- **Token check.** The decision must be `Allow`, and `verifier.consume(token, intent.hash)` must be true. Otherwise it raises `NotAllowedError` and audits `executor.refused`. A forged Allow, a reused token, or a token issued for different args is refused.
- **Approvals are single use.** An `Allow` with an `approval_id` spends it: `mark_used` runs in the same transaction as the `started` row. An approval that is already used, or whose task or intent hash differs, raises `NotAllowedError` (audited `executor.refused`) and nothing runs. A replayed or ambiguous key doesn't spend it.
- **`recorded(key)`** is for a runner resuming at a pinned key, before it calls the gate: `succeeded` returns the result (`replayed=True`), `started` returns `ambiguous=True`, `failed` returns the definite error, and no row returns None. The approval is spent with `started`, so after a definite failure of an approved call, re-evaluating gives `Deny(APPROVAL_USED)`: a retry needs a new task.
- **What happens to a key**, by its row in `executions`:
  - `succeeded`: the recorded result is returned (`replayed=True`).
  - `started`: the outcome is unknown, so it is **ambiguous** and is never re-run. It stays that way until `resolve_ambiguous` is called.
  - `failed`: a definite failure; it may be retried.
- **Outcomes:**
  - Reads: any error is a definite failure.
  - Write and pay: only `ToolFailed` is definite. A timeout (`spec.timeout_s`), cancellation, unexpected exception, or unserializable result is ambiguous.
- **Never inside a transaction:** `execute()` refuses to run inside a caller's transaction. Its `started` row must be committed before the side effect.
- **Calls in one step:** use a distinct `call_index` for each call within a step. Two calls with the same index and intent are deduped by design.
- **Audit:** `executor.start`, `executor.result`, `executor.ambiguous`, `executor.replayed`, `executor.refused`, and `executor.ambiguous_resolved` are all written.
- **Secrets.** When `intent.spec.secrets` is non-empty and the key isn't already succeeded or started, the executor reads exactly those refs (`secrets.env_for(intent.extension, spec.secrets)`) after the token check and before the `started` row. It is the only Keychain reader for gated calls. A `SecretMissing` or `SecretUnavailable` (or no `SecretStore`) is a definite failure with **no `executions` row**, audited `executor.result`, approval not spent; `ToolResult.notice` is the code-built reply: `Not done: secret github/token is missing. On the Mini: resonant secret set github/token`. The local runner sends `notice` as is. A replay never reads the Keychain.
- **CallContext.** Handlers that subclass `ContextHandler` (extension tools and jobs) get `CallContext(secrets, idempotency_key, task_id, events)`; `secrets` holds only this spec's declared secrets. Plain `(args) -> dict` handlers (builtins) are unchanged and never see secrets. A context handler appends its run's events to `ctx.events`; they come back on `ToolResult.events` (P3-10/P3-12 persist them).
- **Redaction.** Before anything is stored, audited or returned, the executor applies `Redactor(values_of(env))` to the result (`deep`, which also covers values that only appear after JSON decoding), the error, the events and the failure audit's `stderr_tail` (from an `ExtFailed`/`ExtAmbiguous`). The process runner already redacted stdout and stderr; this is the second pass.
- **Failure reason** (PER-268).
