- `VoiceResult.reason`: `ok`, `cached`, `disabled`, `paused`, `limited`, `cap`, `not_set_up`, `timeout`, `refused`, `failed` or `guard:<kind>`. `used` False means `text` is exactly the `fallback`.
- `Components.voice: Voice | None` (set by `build_stack` when `brain.voice.enabled`); `ClaudeRunner(..., voice=)`; `pulse_job.deliver(..., rewrite=)`, `send_pulse(..., voice=)`, `voiced_pulse`, `pulse_facts`; `briefing.send_briefing(..., voice=)`, `briefing_facts`; `ClaudeRunner._report_merged(..., title=)`. `rollup.merged_parts(title)`, `rollup.stall_kind(item, roll)` are public now.
- kv: `voice.calls:<YYYY-MM-DD>` (calls that day, 7 days kept), `voice.out:<ref>` (`{digest, text, used, reason, at}`, 2 days; the digest is of the prompt and the fallback, so changed facts call again). `pulse.inflight:<key>` records gain `voice` (bool).
- Config `brain.voice`: `enabled`, `kinds`, `timeout_s` (1..30, 25), `daily_cap` (0..500, 40; 0 none), `max_chars` (200..900, 600), `model`, `max_budget_usd` (0.10).
- Audit `voice.write {kind, used, reason, calls_today}`. Tests patch `runners.voice.default_claude_cmd` to None (`tests/conftest.py`).
- Eval: `evals/task_eval.py` rows with `kind: voice` (`VoiceCase`, `run_voice_case`, `grade_voice`, `voice_summary`); `e2e/fake_claude.py` one-shot honors `limit`, `delay`, `hang`, `crash` and `init_tools`.

## Daemon wiring: `resonant.wiring`, `resonant.daemon`

```python
build_channels(settings, principals, conn, *, osa_runner=None) -> dict[str, Channel]   # one adapter per enabled channel
build_stack(settings, conn, components, loop, *, daemon_healthy, model=None, osa_runner=None,
            started_at=None, channels: Mapping[str, Channel] | None = None)
    -> Stack(channel: ChannelHub, router, runner, reminders, monitor, model, owner, jobs, claude,
             notifier, pulse, trust, brain)
build_imessage_stack(...) / IMessageStack                         # the pre-PER-292 names, aliases
route_event(router_route, notifier, pulse=None) -> router hook   # ext.<n>.item to Pulse.route, other ext:* to Notifier.route, the rest to Router.route
Stack.start(loop) / .stop() / .aclose() / .running
local_step_limit(tools: Iterable[ToolSpec] = ()) -> float   # chat tools; ext tools capped at 300s; no model term (PER-256)
brain_step_limit(model_cfg, tools=()) -> float            # local_step_limit(tools) + model.timeout_s
Daemon(settings, *, model: ModelClient | None = None, osa_runner: OsaRunner | None = None,
       ops_runner: OpsRunner | None = None, secrets: SecretStore | None = None,
       ext_runner: ExtRunner | None = None, channels: Mapping[str, Channel] | None = None)
Daemon.stack: Stack | None                                # Daemon.imessage: the same, pre-PER-292 name
ChannelsConfig.enabled() -> tuple[str, ...]               # enabled channel names, iMessage first
```

- **Gate.** Always `PolicyGate`, channels on or off. The startup log line (`daemon.started_line`) includes `gate=policy dry_run=<bool>`; a non-default instance's starts `instance <name> (<display_name>), ` (PER-297). With `dry_run: true`, L0 reads (chat and the fast path) are allowed exactly as before.
- **Every channel disabled** (the default, and no `channels=` given): the daemon is Phase 0 apart from the gate. `loop.router` is None, `loop.runners == {"echo"}`, `DaemonState.monitor` is None, and nothing imports the model client.
- **Any channel enabled** (PER-292: `settings.channels.enabled()`, or adapters passed as `Daemon(channels=...)`, e.g. `FakeChannel("slack")` in tests; Cori runs with iMessage off): `Daemon.__init__` calls `build_stack` once, after `build_components`. It builds the adapters (`IMessageChannel(config, principals, conn, runner=osa_runner)` when iMessage is on) and one `ChannelHub` over them, which every component below gets as its `channel`, `Router(..., channels=hub.names)` over the components' registry, gate and handlers, registers `LocalRunner` (`components.register_local_runner(..., timezone=settings.timezone)`, then `loop.runners["local"]`, `max_step_s = local_step_limit`) and `ReminderRunner` (`runners["reminder"]`, see Reminders), builds the `Notifier` (same recipients as `JobRunner`, `config=extensions.notify`), sets `loop.router = route_event(router.route, notifier)`, and builds `HealthMonitor(conn, model, channel=hub, channels=hub.adapters, channel_failures=lambda: hub.failures, owner, daemon_healthy=Daemon.healthy, db_path, model_meta, ext_checks=ExtensionHealth(...))`, which becomes `DaemonState.monitor`. It also builds `TrustChecks` (`Stack.trust`), the `AskRunner` (`runners["ask"]`, BR-5), the `ClaudeRunner` (`Stack.claude`, `runners["claude"]`, `max_runs = claude.max_concurrent − claude.reserved_oncall_slots`, at least 1; the router's relay gets it for owner replies) and the `Pulse` (`Stack.pulse`). `model` defaults to `OllamaClient(settings.model)` (closed by `aclose`); `osa_runner` defaults to real osascript. Tests and `resonant bench e2e` inject `resonant.e2e.ScriptedModel` and `RecordingOsaRunner`; tests also pass `ops_runner=FakeOpsRunner()` (it goes to `build_components`).
- **Start:** `Daemon.run` starts the hub (`hub.start(loop.submit)`: every adapter; if none starts, the daemon exits 1; then `ops.back_up`: `Back up (<commit>), down Ns` once after a requested restart, then `extensions.host.send_alerts`: queued tamper alerts to the owner, once each) before the loop, then the loop, the API, the monitor, the notifier's flush (`notifier.run()`) and the dead-man (with `monitor.deadman_reason`). `Stack.stop` stops the flush with the monitor.
- **Jobs.** `build_stack` also registers `JobRunner` as `runners["job"]` (recipients: `PolicyGate.approvers`). `Daemon.__init__` then builds the `Scheduler` (`channel` is the hub or None) and sets `loop.scheduler`; `Daemon.run` calls `scheduler.start()` before the loop, then (when any extension has MCP servers) starts `host.start_mcp()` as a task so pin drift is found and alerted early. With every channel off, L0 and core jobs still run, and a gated job task fails as `unknown runner`. The startup line includes `jobs=<count>`.
- **Stop** (SIGTERM/SIGINT, or a component exiting): the API is told to exit; the monitor and the adapters (the chat.db reader) stop first, so no new events arrive; the scheduler's inline runs are cancelled (`aclose`); then the loop drains in-flight steps (10s grace; a draining step can still send its reply); then every MCP server's group is ended (`host.aclose()`); then the model client closes and the store closes. Unfinished steps resume from their checkpoints on the next start, and the send dedupe key (`reply:<task id>`) prevents a double reply.

## Spans and logs: `resonant.observability`

- `with span(conn, name, trace_id=..., task_id=..., parent_id=..., **attrs) as s: s.set(...)` writes a row to `spans` with status and timing.
- Logs are JSON lines in `~/.resonant/logs/resonant.jsonl`, rotated at 20MB × 5. Standard-library loggers flow through structlog.
- `httpx` and `httpcore` are kept at WARNING because request URLs can carry credentials, such as a secret in the dead-man URL.

## CLI exit codes

- `resonant status`: 0 when the daemon is up, 1 when it is down (in both text and `--json` modes). It never creates the store. Text mode shows `backup      <last_ok_at> (<n>h ago)` (or `none yet`, plus `last try FAILED at …` after a newer failure), then a health section: one line per check (state, since, and the detail when degraded).
- `resonant backup now` (BK-1): one backup (`run_backup`, `requested_by=owner:cli`), safe while the daemon runs, allowed while paused; 0 ok (prints the path, size and time, pruned names, the extra copy), 1 on failure (older copies kept) or when the store doesn't exist (never created). `resonant backup list`: the last result from kv (store opened read-only, skipped if missing), the daily and `extra_dir` copies (name, size, `weekly` on the Sundays retention keeps) and any `pre-restore` copies; 0. `resonant backup restore <file> [--yes]`: 1 and the steps (stop with `resonant daemon uninstall --no-ollama --no-keep-awake`, restore, `resonant daemon install`, `resonant status`) while the daemon runs (its API answers, launchd has `com.resonant.daemon` loaded, or a process of this user runs `resonant daemon run`); 1 for a file `verify_file` refuses; otherwise asks (unless `--yes`), saves the current store to `backups/pre-restore/`, restores (paused), and prints the next steps; 0. `backup now` exits 1 with `not now: another backup is running` when the lock is held.
- `resonant approvals list [--json]`, `show <id|code> [--json]`, `approve <id|code>`, `deny <id|code>`: 0 on success, 1 when nothing matches or the gate refuses (already resolved, expired, task no longer waiting), 75 when the store is missing or its schema is behind. They never create or migrate the store; `list` and `show` open it read-only. `list` shows pending approvals the owner can approve (code, tool, expires in, summary, id). `show` adds the full args from the `gate.evaluate` audit row, which the summary masks. An id is tried first; codes are reused over time, so a code matches only a currently pending approval. `approve` and `deny` print the summary, then call `PolicyGate.resolve(ApprovalResolution(actor=<owner>, actor_identity="cli", path="cli"))`, which resolves and wakes the task in one transaction; the running daemon picks the task up on its next tick.
- `resonant job-guard`: 75 when paused or when the state is unknown, 127 when exec fails, 2 when no command is given.
- `resonant selftest imessage [--timeout S] [--json]`: 0 when the self-test passes, 1 otherwise. It sends a real iMessage to `self_handle`, stores kv `imessage.selftest`, and is safe to run while the daemon is up. macOS grants Automation per responsible app, so running it from a terminal proves the terminal's grant, not the daemon's.
- `resonant eval task [--set] [--model live|scripted] [--repeat N] [--only ID|CATEGORY] [--route triage|claude] [--live-claude] [--limit N] [--json] [--min-accuracy 0.85] [--out DIR]` (BR-6, `resonant.evals.task_eval`; format and metrics in [`evals/README.md`](../evals/README.md#task-eval)): 0 when the accuracy ≥ `--min-accuracy`, 1 below, 2 for a bad set or option. Each case runs `run_case(case, RecordingModel(model), base=, timeout_s=)`: a temp home (`eval_settings`: everything under it, `dry_run` off, `ops.repo_path` None, `brain.read_roots` inside it), `seed_world` (repos, runs and transcripts, files, notes, reminders, facts, `FIXTURES` extensions via `install_fixture`, history as done tasks), `build_components(..., ops_runner=FakeOpsRunner(), secrets=SecretStore(FakeKeychain()))` with `components.executor = EvalExecutor(...)` (reads and `REAL_WRITES` run; any other allowed side effect is recorded in `.stubbed` and returns `{"eval": "not run"}`), `build_stack` with a `FakeChatDB` and `RecordingOsaRunner`, and `EvalAsk` as `runners["ask"]` (records, queues nothing). The outcome comes from the case's first task (checkpoint reply, `Done.result`), its trace's `runner.local.tool` spans (gated `NeedsApproval` = asked, `Allow` = ran) and `runner.ask.guard` spans (kind `numbers`: the number-guard hits), and the Claude bridge's `runner.ask.mcp_call` spans (the tools called; `rounds` is their count). The summary keeps `modes: {loop: ...}` so saved runs stay comparable; the `script` row key (qwen's tool rounds) is accepted and ignored. `grade(case, outcome) -> [failure codes]`; `summarize(results)` has no texts; `save_summary(..., prefix="task-")`. BR-10: `--route` is `brain.route` for the run (default `triage`; `run(route=)`, `eval_settings(..., route)`); rows may carry `expect.route` (`local|claude`, graded `routed:claude` / `routed:local`), `triage` (`{handler, kind?, message_type?, say?}`: the scripted model's triage answer, default claude) and `claude` (`{reads?, calls?: [{name, args}], answer?}`: the real `AskRunner` with `tool_access` over `e2e/fake_claude.py`, a fake Keychain token). A `guarded` ask (the fallback replaced Claude's answer) counts as answered. A handed-over turn with no scripted Claude (live runs, `EvalAsk`) is graded on routing, unapproved writes and `tools_not` only (`delegated`); a scripted one on Claude's answer and its bridge calls (`runner.ask.mcp_call` spans). `Outcome` adds `route`, `handoff`, `triage`, `claude_reply`; `summarize` adds `route: {claude, local, rate_claude, by_handoff, accuracy_claude, accuracy_local, kept_wrong, kept_wrong_rate, delegated, triage_graded, triage_correct, triage_accuracy, ack_ms}`.
- `resonant model probe` and `resonant model bench`: 1 when the probe fails (model missing, context too small, or thinking not off). `bench` also exits 1 when every call of a kind failed. `probe` stores its result in kv `model.probe` if the store exists, and never creates it.
- `resonant bench e2e [--n N] [--model fake|real] [--json]`: 0 when every prompt got a reply and each path's p50 meets its target (`fast` ≤ 1000 ms, `route` ≤ 3000 ms: a greeting through the brain's triage; PER-256 dropped `llm` and `loop`), 1 otherwise, 2 for an unknown `--model`. `fast` and `llm` run with `brain.mode: intents`; `loop` (BR-6) gets its own throwaway home with `brain.mode: loop` (the fake model lists one directory, then answers), its runner dirs and read roots inside the home and `dry_run` on. It runs the production reader, loop, router, runner and channel in a throwaway home with a `FakeChatDB` and a `RecordingOsaRunner`, timing each prompt from the chat.db row insert to the osascript call (N per path, after one untimed warm-up per path). `--model fake` (default, CI) uses `ScriptedModel`; `--model real` uses the configured Ollama model. It never texts anyone and never touches `~/.resonant/resonant.db`.
- `resonant secret set <ref>`: reads the value from a hidden prompt (asked twice) on a terminal, or one line of stdin otherwise, never from argv; stores it, reads it back and compares (a mismatch removes the item). 0 stored, 1 the entries or the read-back didn't match, 2 a bad ref or value, 75 the Keychain is unavailable. `resonant secret check <ref>`: a no-prompt read; prints `ok (no prompt)` and never the value; 0 ok, 1 missing, 75 unavailable. `resonant secret list [DIR…]`: the known refs (PER-313: `secret_expiry.known_refs`), every ref with an expiry row, and the secrets declared by installed extensions (`~/.resonant/extensions/*/resonant.yaml`) and any given extension dirs, each `present` or `missing` with its expiry (no value is read); 75 if the Keychain is unavailable. `resonant secret set <ref> [--expires YYYY-MM-DD | --expires-in <N>d]` and `resonant secret expiry <ref> (--expires … | --expires-in … | --clear)`: see [Secrets](#secrets-resonantsecrets-resonantredact) (PER-313). `resonant secret rm <ref>`: 0 removed, 1 not found, 75 unavailable.
- `resonant ext panel-history <ext> <panel> [--rebuild [--since YYYY-MM-DD]]` (PER-286): lists a history panel's archived days (day, status, bytes, tries, last error); `--rebuild` queues every kept day (or those from `--since`, which must be inside `keep` and already ended) as `pending` again for the daemon to re-archive one at a time, keeping each old copy until then; audited `panel.history_rebuild`. 0 on success, 1 for an unknown extension or a panel without history or a `--since` outside the range, 2 for a malformed `--since` or `--since` without `--rebuild`. Owner-run; Claude may run it when asked.
- `resonant ext add <source> [--adopt]`, `ext update <name>`, `ext remove <name> [--purge]`: 0 on success, 1 when refused (bad source, failed checks, wrong typed name, leftovers without `--adopt`, pins changed since the confirmation, not installed), 75 when the store is missing or behind or the Keychain is unavailable. `ext list [--json]`: name, status, source@commit, digest, whether the copy still matches (`ok`, `changed`, `missing`), tool and job counts. `ext validate <dir|file>`: 0 ok, 1 on any schema, name, directory or approver problem.
- `resonant notify-owner test [--clear]` (PER-301, owner-run: it texts the owner): on an instance with `notify_owner`, POSTs the fixed test alert (`key notify-owner/test`, `TEST_TITLE`; `--clear` its all-clear) to personal Resonant with this instance's token. 0 when personal Resonant took it (prints `texted`, `held (<why>)`, `already told` or `stored, but no channel took the text`), 1 unreachable or refused (401 `notify token mismatch`, 404 `not set up there`, 422), 2 without `notify_owner` in the config, 75 when the Keychain can't be read.
- `resonant eval router` is gone (PER-256).

## Local API

All endpoints are loopback only. Remote status goes through the push-only status relay ([Status relay](#status-relay-resonantdashboardpusher-supabasemigrations-db-3), go-live step 15), never by exposing the API. Besides the loopback bind (config validation), every request must come from a loopback client and carry exactly one `Host` header naming `localhost` or a loopback IP (any port); anything else gets 403 `{"detail": "loopback only"}`. This stops a page that rebinds its DNS name to 127.0.0.1 from reading the API through the owner's browser. Every response carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (`api.SECURITY_HEADERS`). The dashboard build has no inline script, and a test checks that.

| Endpoint | Returns |
|---|---|
| `GET /healthz` | `{"ok": true}` |
| `GET /api/dashboard` | the dashboard snapshot, `local` tier (schema 2; [`dashboard-plan.md`](dashboard-plan.md) §3; the exact shape is [`snapshot.schema.json`](snapshot.schema.json)), `Cache-Control: no-store`. See [Dashboard snapshot](#dashboard-snapshot) |
| `GET /…` (anything else) | the dashboard build in `dashboard/dist`: static files, `index.html` at `/`, and its `config.json` (`{"source": "local"}`). The API routes win over files of the same name. Without a build, `/` is a placeholder page saying it is missing. The mount is decided at start, so a new build needs a restart |
| `GET /api/status` | version, `instance: {name, display_name}` (PER-297), uptime, last tick, dry_run, autonomy, paused, task counts by status and wait reason, `approvals_pending` (unexpired pending approvals, all approvers), in-flight count, `health: {model, imessage, loop, store, ext:<name>…}` each `{state: unknown\|ok\|degraded, since, detail, checked_at}` |
| `GET /api/channels` | `[{name: "imessage", enabled, ok, last_read_at, last_selftest: {ok, at, detail} \| null}]`; `ok` and `last_read_at` are null without a live channel. Then one row per other live adapter (PER-292) with `last_selftest` null; Slack's adds `status: {connected, last_event_at, followed_threads}` (PER-295) |
| `GET /api/ext/<name>/panels/<id>/days/<YYYY-MM-DD>` | PER-286: one archived day of a history panel, read-only (`Cache-Control: no-store`): `{ext, panel, day, views, health, truncated, bytes, outside}`, every text through the snapshot's text pass again and no `local_url`. 404 for an unknown extension, panel (or one without `history`), a malformed day, and a day that was never archived, recorded none, or failed. Other methods: 405 |
| `GET /api/instances` | PER-306: `{instances: [{name, display_name, self}]}`: this daemon first (`self: true`), then each of `settings.instances` (`display_name` the name capitalized; the page takes the real one from the snapshot). Without `instances:` config it lists only this daemon, and the page shows no switcher |
| `GET /api/instances/<name>/dashboard` | PER-306, the loopback proxy: a linked instance's `GET <url>/api/dashboard` (`InstanceLink.url`, loopback http only; 5 s timeout, no redirects followed, no environment proxy, at most 8 MiB), returned with `strip_local_json` applied (below) and `Cache-Control: no-store`, so the page stays same-origin under `connect-src 'self'`. 404 for a name not in `instances:`; 502 `{detail, since}` while it doesn't answer (`<name> isn't answering`), answers non-200 (`<name> answered HTTP 500`, a redirect included), sends no JSON object, or names another instance (`<url> answers as resonant, not cori`): `since` is the first failure since its last answer (kept in memory, `DaemonState.instance_down`). Read-only: the one GET |
| `GET /api/tools` | registered tool specs: `[{name, extension, effect, level, description, args_schema}]` (`level` is the effective level) |
| `POST /api/notify-owner` | PER-301, the notify-owner bridge ([below](#notify-owner-bridge-resonantnotify_owner-per-301)): another instance's urgent alert, texted to the owner as personal Resonant's own. `Authorization: Bearer <token>` matching `core/notify_token_<instance>` in this daemon's Keychain; body exactly `{instance, key ≤120 ([a-z0-9/._:-]), title ≤200, urgency: "urgent", clear: bool}` (≤2048 bytes). 200 `{outcome: sent\|held\|told\|unreachable, reason}`; 401 missing or wrong token (`{"detail": "notify token mismatch"}`); 422 any other body (a title with nothing printable after `clean_text` included; a `Content-Length` over the cap is refused before the body is read); 404 no token for that instance, the daemon's own name, or no channel stack; 503 Keychain or channel unavailable (stored untold, the retry sends). A refusal stores nothing |

### Notify-owner bridge: `resonant.notify_owner` (PER-301)

```python
NotifyOwnerConfig(url="http://127.0.0.1:7777", token_ref="core/notify_token", timeout_s=5.0)
    # Settings.notify_owner (None: no forwarding). Loopback http(s) base URL only; refused on the
    # default instance (personal Resonant texts the owner; it never forwards to itself)
NotifyOwnerBody(instance, key, title, urgency: Literal["urgent"], clear: bool = False)  # strict, extra=forbid
parse_body(raw: bytes) -> NotifyOwnerBody | str          # str: the 422 reason
token_ref_for(instance) -> "core/notify_token_<instance, - as _>" | None
owner_key(raw) -> str                                    # a valid pulse key (long ones hashed)
await post_alert(cfg, token, body, *, transport=None) -> Delivery(status | None, outcome, reason)
await send_test(cfg, instance, secrets, *, clear=False) -> Delivery    # `resonant notify-owner test`
OwnerBridge(conn, cfg, *, instance, secrets, channel=None, owner=None, clock=utcnow, transport=None)
    .forward(key, title, *, clear=False) -> None   # sync, never raises; kv outbox, latest per key wins
    await .flush() -> int                          # due entries once, oldest first; delivered count
    await .run() / .stop()                         # the daemon's "notify-owner" task (Stack.bridge)
    .pending() -> {key: entry}; .next_due_s() -> float
```

- **Outbox** (kv `notify_owner.outbox`): `{key: {title, clear, seq, queued_at, next_at, attempts}}`, at most 50 (the oldest dropped), an entry older than 24 h dropped undelivered. A flush writes back only entries whose `seq` is unchanged, so a `forward` during the POST is kept.
- **Answers.** 2xx: removed (audit `notify_owner.sent`). 422: dropped (audit `notify_owner.refused`, a bug: retrying can't help). Anything else (unreachable, 401, 404, 5xx) or no token here: that entry and the rest of the due ones back off 5 s doubling to 300 s.
- **One DM per problem** (kv `notify_owner.down` `{problem, since, told}`): `unreachable`, `mismatch` (401: "notify token mismatch: …"), `not_set_up` (404) or `no_token`, sent to the owner on this instance's own channel with `dedupe_key="notify-owner:<problem>:<since>"`; a failed DM is tried on the next flush, a refused one counts as told. A delivery clears it.
- **Callers** (all only with `notify_owner` set): `Pulse.route` (above), `HealthMonitor` (an incident that reaches `alert_after`: `forward("health/<check>", "<check>: <detail>")`, its recovery `clear=True`; kv `notify_owner.health` lists the forwarded checks; independent of whether this instance's own channel can text) and `ClaudeRunner` (a run's `failed` end at level `now`: `forward("run/<code>", "<code> failed: <why>")`).
- **Never logged at INFO:** titles (message bodies).

### Dashboard snapshot

`resonant.dashboard.snapshot` (DB-1, rewritten by PER-276: schema 2). `build_snapshot(conn, settings, *, started_at, last_tick, slots, runner_missing, now, version, core_jobs, redactor, launch_agents, diagram, owner, slack, secret_facts) -> Snapshot` reads the store only (the same store queries as `/api/status`, `/runs`, `/pulse`, `/trust` and `ext list`) and returns the `local` tier; `to_remote(snapshot)` returns the `remote` tier. `core_jobs` is the scheduler's `(id, cron, tz)` per core job (`api.local_snapshot` passes it; without it `core_jobs[]` lists the store's job states with no schedule); `redactor` is a `redact.Redactor` of secret values every text must also lose (the pusher passes its token and key). The models are the contract: `docs/snapshot.schema.json` is generated from them (`uv run python -m resonant.dashboard.snapshot > docs/snapshot.schema.json`) and a test fails when it is stale. `dashboard/src/types.ts` mirrors it (and `dashboard/src/lib/legacy.ts` keeps the schema-1 types the page still reads from an old daemon's push).

- **Tiers (PER-276; this supersedes the text-free remote tier of plan D3).** The tiers carry the same data. The remote tier is stored in Supabase, where only the owner (password and TOTP, `aal2`) can read it, so it shows what the local page shows: asks, summaries, approval and question texts, pulse titles, health reasons, tool commands, trust rules, repos. Exactly two things stay on the Mini, each declared with `LOCAL_ONLY` (`"x-tier": "local"` in the schema), optional and left out of the JSON when null: `remote_url` (a running worker's, `RunItem`, and the Claude brain's, `ClaudeBrain`: the Remote Control link, which lets whoever holds it drive the session) and an approval's `code` (`ApprovalItem`: the second factor an L1 yes needs). `to_remote` clears them at any depth; a test lists every `LOCAL_ONLY` field and checks that a remote dump is the local one with only those removed. The page shows no placeholder for them.
- **Text: one pass, capped, with provenance.** Every free text passes `redact.clip_text(value, cap, known=redactor)` once: credential-shaped text is replaced (`redact_secrets` over the first `cap` + 256 characters, so a secret straddling the cut is seen whole), then the caller's known secret values, then the text is cut to `cap` characters ending with `… [cut]` (the marker counted inside the cap). The caps are in the field types (`maxLength` in the schema; nothing is over 800; a test checks every free-text node of the schema and random 0-5000 character unicode through every source): `ask` 300, `summary` 800, `failure` 400, `last_note` 200, `did[]` and effect `what` 80, `merged[]` 40, a flagged path 120, `branch` 120, `repo` 100, an approval `summary` 300 and a batch change 200, a question 600 and an option 80, a pulse title 200 and key 120, a health `reason` 200, a task `label` 120 and `error` 300, a brain `command` 200 and `cwd` 120, a trust rule 200, a runner `missing` entry 120, an extension `description` 120, a tool `description` 160, a cron 64. A model that has text from outside the owner's conversation (a worker, an extension, a web page) lists the names of those fields in `outside` (default `[]`), so the page can say so: a run's `summary`, `failure`, `last_note`, `did`, `effects` and `flagged_paths` when present, and `ask` when its task is tainted (`system_texts.tainted(checkpoint)`); an approval's `["summary", "changes"]` when its task is tainted (as `runners/approve.py` marks the system text); a question's `["text", "options"]`; a pulse item's `["title"]` unless it is a core `notice/` item (as `pulse_job.is_outside`); a task's `["error"]`; a brain tool's `["command"]`; a health row's `["reason"]` for an `ext:<name>` check. **A member's words** (PER-304): a task whose `principal` is not the owner's (a member, or the Slack workspace's default principal on Cori, so every Slack member's message there) has its task `["label", "title"]`, its run's `ask` and its approvals' `["summary", "changes"]` marked, through the same pass and the pusher's second scan; `build_snapshot(..., owner=)` names the owner (`api.local_snapshot` passes `principals.owner` when principals.yaml is loaded; without it nothing is marked for this). A Remote Control link inside any text is replaced by `[remote control link]` (`redact.scrub_rc_links`, also in the pusher's dump scan; every `remote_url` held is a known value too) and `relay_ingest` refuses the shape (`RC_LINK_SQL`). The brain's tool calls do ship their `cwd` (at most 120 characters, as the local page shows it) and `command`. What is never shipped at all: task checkpoints, results, scopes, channel refs, session ids and task `worktree_path`s; a run's workspace; run commands; MCP pins and input schemas; an extension's source path; secret values (only names). A `pr_url` is kept only when it matches `^https://github\.com/[\w.-]+/[\w.-]+/pull/\d+$`; an MCP http server shows its host only; `head_sha` matches `^[0-9a-f]{7,40}$`.
- **Relay checks.** The relay refuses what still gets through: `relay_ingest` rejects secret-shaped text (`redact.SECRET_SQL_FAMILY`, a Postgres regex; `redact_secrets` is a superset of it, a test fuzzes that) and the pusher scans the dump once more for its two secrets and for credential-shaped text (see [Status relay](#status-relay-resonantdashboardpusher-supabasemigrations-db-3)).
- **Fail soft.** A health row, extension or job whose identifier doesn't match is left out, and counts keep only the known task and run states, so one stale row never breaks the endpoint. A runner check that raises shows as one `missing` entry.
- **Shape (`schema: 2`).** `schema, tier, taken_at, version, started_at, last_tick, autonomy, paused, dry_run` as before; `trimmed` (`none|history|panels|lists|detail|items`: how far the pusher cut the lists, below) and `limits {runs: 50, run_detail: 20, tasks: 50, task_detail: 20, approvals_recent: 20, approvals_pending: 20, questions: 20, pulse: 60, brain_tools: 20}` (the page quotes them: "it keeps the newest 50 runs"); `health[] {name, state, since, reason, outside}`; `tasks {by_state, items[]}`; `slots`; `runs {by_state, detected, items[]}`; `approvals {open, items[], recent[]}`; `questions {open, items[]}`; `pulse {open, untold, items[]}`; `extensions[]` (cap 20); `core_jobs[]` (cap 12); `runner {set_up, missing[] (cap 10), gaps}`; `trust_rules[]` and `repos[]` (cap 50 each; `trust_rule_count` and `repo_count` stay); `claude_brain {state, turns, remote_url?, tools[]} | null`; `brain_activity {calls_today, writes_today, state, turns}`. The `brief` lists and `brain_activity.recent` of schema 1 are gone: the rows they stood for are the real rows now.
  - `tasks.items[]` (`task_store.recent`: active first, then newest; 50): `{id, kind, runner, extension, status, wait_reason, wake_at, attempts, step_no, created_at, updated_at, label, error, run_code, history[], approval_ids[], outside}`. `label` is the checkpoint's `title`, else `ext/job` for a job (code-built); `history` the last 10 `audit_log` rows of the task (`audit_task` index; `{at, event}` with the action as the event, only for the first 20 tasks); `approval_ids` the task's approvals that are in `approvals` (at most 10).
  - `runs.items[]` (`runs.recent(limit=50)`, active first): `{code, state, waiting_reason (only while waiting), started_at, ended_at, detected, repo, ask, branch, base_branch, head_sha, pr_url, pr_number, github_repo (owner/name from the repo registry; null for a scratch run), created_at, remote_url (local only; only while running), task_id, step, attempts, sessions, exit_code, cost_usd, tokens_in, tokens_out, session_seconds, wait_seconds, files_changed, flagged_paths[] (5), summary, failure, last_note, did[] (10, newest last), effects[] {kind, what, high} (10), merged[] (5), merged_by (resonant|run|null), history[] (12, oldest first; `{at, event}` from the run's `run.*` audit rows with the `run.` dropped), approval_ids[] (10), question_ids[] (open asks), linear_ids[] (ids like `PER-276` found in `ask` and `branch`, at most 5, as plain text: no workspace is configured to link them), detail, outside}`. Past the first 20 runs `detail` is false and `summary, failure, did, effects, history, flagged_paths` are empty. A run has no turns (only the Claude brain does): the page shows sessions, attempts, cost, tokens and seconds. `audit.run_events` is bounded: it starts at a floor (the oldest `run.start` of the listed runs' tasks; for a run with no task, the first audit row at or after its `created_at`) and reads at most 20000 matching rows, newest first, so a busy log can cut a run's oldest events.
  - `approvals.items[]` are every unexpired pending approval (oldest first, at most 20), `approvals.recent[]` those decided (approved, rejected or expired) in the last 7 days (newest decision first, at most 20), each `{id, member_ids[], code (local only), summary, level, effect, needs_code, tool, extension, status, asked_at, expires_at, decided_at, decided_by, task_id, run_code, changes[] | null (10), outside}`. A batch (BA-1, PER-254) is one row where its first approval is: `id` that approval's id, `member_ids` all of them in order (the page matches a link to any), the batch's code (its first approval's), the summary `N changes`, its strictest level and effect, `needs_code` as the gate decides it (any approval needs it, or a change widens trust), `asked_at` the batch prompt's time, its one expiry, and `changes: [{summary, level}]` (each change's `change_line`, in order; null on a lone approval). A batch whose approvals aren't all in the list any more can't be answered as one, so what is left of it shows as lone rows. `approvals.open` counts pending rows, so a batch counts once (`/api/status`'s `approvals_pending` still counts approvals).
  - `questions.items[]` (the open asks of active runs, oldest first, at most 20): `{id, run_code, kind, text, options[] (6), urgent, asked_at, outside}`. `pulse.items[]` (most urgent first, then newest; at most 60): `{source, key, title, urgency, state, updated_at, expires_at, told_at, run_code (from a core `run/<code>` key), outside}`, `state` `new` (the owner hasn't heard this version) or `told`.
  - `extensions[]`: `{name, status, health (its ext:<name> check), muted_until, jobs[], description, source_kind (repo|path|other), commit (12 hex), installed_at, tools[] {name, via (cli|mcp), server, effect, level, description} (40), mcp_servers[] {id, transport, host} (5), secrets[] (names, 10), report_max, repos[] (20)}`; a job is `{id, schedule, tz, effect, level, next_due, last_run_at, last_outcome, fail_streak}` (`next_due` comes from the approved manifest's cron, null when the extension is disabled or the schedule doesn't parse; `last_outcome` is one of the scheduler's outcomes or `other`); `core_jobs[]` are the same shape for the daemon's own jobs (effect `read`, level `L0`). `repos[]` is `{name, mode, base, ci, slug}` (`ci`: CI seen, null when not checked); `trust_rules[].n` is the rule id.
  - `extensions[].panels[]` (PER-280, manifest order, at most 8; the models are flat on purpose, no recursion): `{id, title, description, schedule (the idle cron), active, tz, updated_at (the data's time), last_run_at (null: never run), ok, stale, error, fail_streak, health (the author's ok|warn|bad), truncated, bytes, local_url (local tier only), views[], outside}` with `views[] {id, title, widgets[]}` and widgets `stat | series | table | status | kv | text | details | price` discriminated by `type` (fields in `docs/snapshot.schema.json`; unset optional fields are left out of the JSON, a required null such as a stat's `value` stays). A `series` time axis is unix seconds, a table's cells are aligned to its `columns` (`rows: [[cell]]`), a `details` widget is `rows[] {depth, key, kind, value}`, and a `price` widget (PER-282) is columnar and delta-coded: `{mode: candles|line, t0, dt[], dp, q, o[], h[], l[], c[], trades[], markers[], aggregated_from}` (encoding in `docs/extensions.md` §6a). `stale` is true when `fail_streak > 0`, there is no good copy, the extension isn't `enabled`, or `now > expect_by`. `outside` lists `views` (when it has views) and `error` (when set): the extension's own words. Every string of a stored copy passes the one text pass again at build; a copy that no longer validates shows no views and `error: "stored copy unreadable"`. `PANEL_BUDGET_BYTES` (100 KB of compact view JSON across all panels) is enforced by the builder (`fit_panels` over the dump, `_fit_models` over the models): the largest panel first, the oldest data among equals, then `(ext, id)` loses its `views`, gets `truncated`, and `views` leaves `outside`. `to_remote` clears `local_url` like any local-only field.
  - `diagram.nodes[]` gains `kind: "panel"` (PER-280): `id` `panel:<ext>/<id>`, `parent` `ext:<ext>`, `label` the title, `text` the description (else "`<ext>/<id>`: a dashboard panel; Resonant runs its panel command <refresh words> and draws the result."), `tags` `["panel","read","L0"]` plus `stale`, `health` (`unknown` never run, `failing` at 3 failures in a row, `degraded` when stale or not ok, else `ok`), `last_run_at` = the data's time, `ext`, `panel`; one edge `ext:<ext>` → panel labelled `draws`. The pusher's diagram trims treat `panel` like `tool`, `server`, `repo` and `job`.
  - `health[].state` allows `failing` for the relay (DB-3); the monitor writes only `ok`, `degraded` and `unknown`.
- **Untold, mutes and detected side effects (PER-263).** `pulse.untold` counts the items whose latest version the owner hasn't heard: `pulse.pending` at every urgency, so muted sources and cleared items whose last word is owed are in it (`pulse.open` counts uncleared, unexpired items, told or not). `extensions[].muted_until` is `notify.muted_until` (null when not muted). `runs.detected` is `{by_kind: {<side_effects.Kind>: n}, high: n}` over the side effects detected (TR-1, kv `run.seen:<code>`) in the commands of the runs `runs.items` lists (the last 50); `high` counts the high-impact ones (a base push, a publish, a delete). `runs.items[].detected` is that run's count. `EffectKind` mirrors `runners.side_effects.Kind` (an import cycle stops a reuse; a test keeps them equal; an unknown stored kind is left out).
- **Size.** Typical (about 40 runs, 33 tasks, 5 extensions, a few approvals and pulse items) is about 118 KB with the diagram, and two full panels of about 24 KB add about 57 KB (PER-280); the panel budget is 100 KB; every list full at every cap with every text at its cap is about 760 KB (twenty extensions with 40 tools each are about half of it). `pusher.MAX_BYTES` is 240 KiB; `pusher.shrink(data)` cuts a bigger dump in fixed steps, each including the ones before, stopping at the first that fits and setting `trimmed`: (1) `history`: a run's last 4 events, 3 did notes and 3 effects, a task's last 3 events, a batch's first 3 changes; (2) `panels` (PER-280): extension panels' views dropped one panel at a time, largest first (oldest data among equals), until it fits; each keeps its state and says `truncated`; (3) `lists`: runs, tasks, recently decided approvals, pulse items, brain tool calls and questions halved together (floor 5 each) until it fits; (4) `detail`: every run's summary, failure, did, effects, history and flagged paths, every task's error and history, every extension's tools and every batch's changes dropped (`detail` false); (5) `items`: all of those lists, the pending approvals, trust rules and repos emptied (counts, health, extensions and jobs stay). No step touches `secrets` (PER-315: at most 16 names and dates). Still too large: the push fails fatally ("snapshot too large"). Every step's output is a valid snapshot (a test checks each).
- **The Claude brain (PER-258).** `claude_brain.tools[]` is `{at, tool, decision, command, cwd, status, write, outside}` from the `brain.tool` audit rows (both tiers, the last 20). `brain_activity` is `{calls_today, writes_today}` over the last 1000 calls, the session's `state` (`up|down`, null before its first start) and `turns`.
- **Live state.** `started_at` (when the daemon started) is required and never null. `slots` is the loop's Claude `SlotPool` (`in_use`, `capacity`). The tick period isn't in the snapshot; the UI's 120 s loop-stall threshold assumes the default `loop.tick_s`. `runner.set_up` comes from `ClaudeRunner.setup_gaps()` (secrets by name, never a value), cached for 60 s by the API; with no Claude runner in the daemon (iMessage off) it is `false`. `version` is the checkout's git sha (8 hex, read once per process), else the package version.
- **Registered jobs, Slack and who asked (PER-306; additive, left out of the JSON when empty, so personal Resonant's dump is unchanged).** `jobs[]` (cap 30, `store.jobs.live`, oldest first): `{id, name, kind (schedule|watch), state (active|paused), schedule (cron), tz, next_due (null while paused or when the cron doesn't parse), last_run_code, last_run_status (that run's state now, null when gone), last_run_at, last_error, created_by (principal name), output (thread|dm|room:<key>), outside}`; `outside` is `["name"]` when the creator isn't the owner. The prompt, memory, seen keys and channel ref never ship. `channels: {slack: {connected, last_event_at, followed_threads}}` from the live Slack adapter's `health()` (`build_snapshot(..., slack=)`, which `api.local_snapshot` reads from `DaemonState.monitor.channels["slack"]`); absent without one. `runs.items[].requested_by` (`Run.requester`) and `approvals.*[].requested_by` (the task's principal) name who asked only when it isn't the owner (none without an `owner`); `decided_by` stays who resolved it.
- **Credential expiries (PER-315; additive, left out of the JSON when empty, so a daemon with none known pushes what it did before; schema stays 2).** `secrets[]` (cap `SECRET_ITEMS` 16, soonest first, then by ref): `{ref, expires_at, days_left, source (owner|assumed|probe), probe (ok|rejected|unknown|null)}`, the same on both tiers. The entries are PER-313's `secret_expiry.entries(known_refs(settings), rows, facts)` that are `watched` (an expiry known and the Keychain item not known to be missing). `facts` is `build_snapshot(..., secret_facts=)`: `api.local_snapshot` passes `SecretsHealth.facts(now)`, the monitor's cached Keychain presence and `mdat` (no `-w`; re-read at most hourly, shared with the `secrets` health row under one lock), found as `DaemonState.monitor.secrets_check`; without a monitor (or on any error) it is None and the stored rows show with presence unknown (no `assumed` default). `days_left` is `secret_expiry.days_left` at `taken_at` in `settings.timezone` (negative once past). `probe` is PER-314's kv `secrets.probe:<ref>` `{state, status, at}` when present (`snapshot.PROBE_KV`), else null. The builder never reads a secret's value; a sentinel test plants one in `FakeKeychain` and checks neither tier carries it.
- **Another instance's snapshot (PER-306).** `strip_local_json(data) -> dict` makes another instance's local-tier JSON safe for this daemon's page: it walks the JSON along `Snapshot`'s models and drops every `LOCAL_ONLY` field (so an approval's `code` goes but a run's `code` stays), drops any `remote_url`/`local_url` key at any depth, and scrubs Remote Control links from every string. It works on plain JSON, so a newer instance's fields this checkout doesn't know pass through (scrubbed), and the page ignores what it doesn't read. A test walks the result for links and approval codes.
- **Read path (dashboard).** Local: `GET /api/dashboard`; with other instances (PER-306), `GET /api/instances` and `GET /api/instances/<name>/dashboard`. Remote: the page lists the instances from `snapshot_latest` (`select instance, display_name:data->instance->>display_name`; personal first; an error, as before PER-304's column, means none) and reads the chosen one's row as the owner (below): `select taken_at … eq(instance)`, and only when it changed `select data, taken_at … eq(instance)`; with no row it falls back to that instance's newest `snapshots` row, and a row whose `data.slim` is true (a history row) is not shown. Panel days filter by `instance` too. With no instance listed it reads the newest row (`order taken_at desc limit 1`), never a bare `maybeSingle` that two rows would break. A schema-1 snapshot (an old daemon's, text-free) is rendered through a legacy adapter with a banner.

## Status relay: `resonant.dashboard.pusher`, `supabase/migrations` (DB-3)

The push-only path from the Mini to Supabase ([`dashboard-plan.md`](dashboard-plan.md) D3–D6). Off by default.

```python
RelayConfig(enabled=False, supabase_url=None, anon_key=None, every_s=60, timeout_s=10, max_attempts=3,
            fly_app=None, fly_path=None)   # PER-316: `resonant relay deploy` (see Fly relay, Deploy)
SnapshotPusher(conn, config: RelayConfig, *, snapshot: Callable[[Redactor], Awaitable[Snapshot]], secrets: SecretStore,
               client=None, sleep=asyncio.sleep, clock=utcnow, monotonic=time.monotonic, jitter=random.random,
               instance="resonant")   # PER-304: sent as p_instance; the daemon passes settings.instance.name
    async def run() -> None             # push_once() every every_s (start to start, ≥ 1 s apart) until cancelled
    async def push_once() -> PushOutcome  # one push with its retries; writes kv health.relay
PushOutcome(ok: bool, fatal: bool, detail: str)
clear_relay_health(conn)                # the daemon calls it when relay.enabled is false
SUPABASE_KEY_REF = "relay/supabase_key" # the ingest token (Keychain)
api.local_snapshot(state, *, redactor=None) -> Snapshot   # what /api/dashboard serves; the pusher sends to_remote() of it
pusher.shrink(data) -> dict             # cut a too-large dump to fit MAX_BYTES (240 KiB); sets data["trimmed"]
```

- **Config.** `relay.supabase_url` is a bare `https://` URL (no credentials, path, query or fragment; not loopback). `relay.anon_key` is the project's anon or publishable key: public by design (the deployed dashboard ships it in `config.json`), and it can neither read nor write on its own. `relay.enabled` needs both. `every_s` is 15–3600.
- **Request.** `POST <supabase_url>/rest/v1/rpc/relay_ingest` with header `apikey: <anon_key>` (plus `Authorization: Bearer <anon_key>` only for a legacy JWT anon key; an `sb_publishable_` key goes on `apikey` alone, as Supabase asks), body `{"p_token": <relay/supabase_key>, "p_data": <remote-tier Snapshot>, "p_instance": <settings.instance.name>}` (PER-304; the snapshot's own `instance` names the same instance, and each instance's token is `relay/supabase_key` under its own Keychain service, `resonant` or `resonant-<name>`). The token is read from the Keychain first on every push (a missing secret fails before anything is built) and is only ever in the body. Redirects aren't followed, proxy and CA environment variables are ignored (`trust_env=False`), and the response body is never read. The snapshot is built with `Redactor([token, anon_key])`, so every text is cleaned of both; `p_data` is `to_remote(...)`, applied inside the pusher, so nothing wider can be passed in, and the dump is then scanned once more: every string and key for the two values and every string value for credential-shaped text (`redact_secrets`). A dump over `MAX_BYTES` (240 KiB as Postgres measures `data::text`; the server cap is 256 KiB) goes through `shrink` (see [Dashboard snapshot](#dashboard-snapshot), Size) and isn't sent if it is still over.
- **Instances** (PER-304). The relay keys every row by instance. The ingest token decides which (`relay_private.ingest_tokens`); a `p_instance` or a snapshot `instance.name` that names another instance is refused with 403, so one daemon can never write another's rows, and each instance's rate limit and row caps are its own. A panel day upload carries `p_instance` too. Personal Resonant (no `instance:` config) sends `resonant`, where every row from before PER-304 already is.
- **Retries.** Up to `max_attempts` per push, each capped at `timeout_s`, with backoff 2 s, 4 s, … (cap 20 s, +0–25 % jitter) for network errors, timeouts, 429 and 5xx. 401/403 (credentials), 404 (migration missing) and other 4xx (refused snapshot) are not retried. A missing Keychain item sends nothing.
- **Health row `relay`.** kv `health.relay` = `{state, since, detail}`, shown as the `relay` row of the snapshot (`detail` is `reason`): `ok` after a push lands, `degraded` after a failed push, `failing` after 3 failed pushes in a row or at once for a non-retryable failure or a missing secret. `detail` is built by code (HTTP status or exception type), redacted of the token and key, and capped at 120 chars. The row in a pushed snapshot is the state *before* that push, so a checker should alert on gaps between snapshots, not on the `relay` row. No row while the relay is off.
- **Kill switch and dry run.** The relay keeps pushing while paused (`/kill`) and under `dry_run`: it is outbound status, not an action, and `paused: true` is what the owner needs to see from away. Only `relay.enabled: false` stops it.
- **`--check` rows** (`scripts/go_live_check.py relay_rows`): `status relay enabled` (advisory), `relay supabase_url set`, `relay anon_key set`, `relay secret relay/supabase_key` (existence only; required when enabled, `SKIP` and the Keychain not asked when off), `relay pushing` (advisory, from kv `health.relay`). Outside `relay_rows`, the advisory `dead-man switch` row (`deadman_row`) passes when `relay.enabled` or `health.healthchecks_url` is set.

### Supabase schema (`supabase/migrations/`)

`20261016000000_relay.sql` (DB-3) creates the tables and functions below; `20261021000000_relay_refuse_claude_brain.sql` (PER-263) made `relay_ingest` also refuse `claude_brain` and a `remote_url` key at any depth; `20261102000000_relay_full_snapshot.sql` (PER-276) adds `snapshot_latest` and replaces `relay_ingest` (it stores the full schema-2 snapshot; the `claude_brain` refusal is gone, the `remote_url` one stays); `20261105000000_relay_panel_days.sql` (PER-286) adds `panel_days` and `relay_panel_day`; `20261120000000_relay_instances.sql` (PER-304) keys the relay by instance: `relay_private.ingest_tokens` (one ingest token per instance; the personal hash moved there as `resonant` and `relay_private.config.ingest_token_sha256` is gone), an `instance` column on all three tables (default `resonant`; the key of `snapshot_latest`, part of the key of `panel_days`), an optional `p_instance` on both write RPCs and an `instances` list from `relay_status`; `20261123000000_relay_secret_expiry.sql` (PER-315) adds `relay_private.status_secrets(jsonb)` (the first 5 of a snapshot's `secrets`, each `{ref, expires_at, days_left}`), puts it in `relay_ingest`'s slim history row as `secrets` (every other check unchanged) and in each `relay_status` `instances[]` entry. Apply them in order; the last one first (before the Fly deploy and the daemon restart), see [go-live step 15](go-live.md).

| Object | Shape | Who |
|---|---|---|
| `public.snapshot_latest` | one row per instance: `instance text primary key default 'resonant'` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$`; PER-304, before it a `singleton` key), `taken_at timestamptz`, `received_at timestamptz default now()`, `data jsonb` (the newest remote-tier snapshot, whole; object; `octet_length(data::text) <= 262144`). RLS on | `SELECT` for `authenticated` when `relay_private.is_owner()`; no other grants or policies |
| `public.snapshots` | `id bigint identity`, `instance text default 'resonant'` (PER-304), `taken_at timestamptz` (the snapshot's own), `received_at timestamptz default now()` (server clock), `data jsonb` (the history: a schema-2 push is stored **slim**, `{schema, tier, taken_at, version, slim: true, health: [{name, state, since}], counts: {runs, tasks, approvals, questions, pulse, untold}}`; a schema-1 push whole; object; `octet_length(data::text) <= 32768`). Indexes on `taken_at desc`, `received_at` and `(instance, taken_at desc)`. RLS on | `SELECT` for `authenticated` when `relay_private.is_owner()`; no other grants or policies |
| `public.panel_days` | PER-286: `instance text default 'resonant'` (PER-304), `ext text`, `panel_id text`, `day date`, `data jsonb` (a history panel's archived views: `{views, health, truncated}`; object; ≤ 64 KiB), `created_at`, `received_at timestamptz default now()`; primary key `(instance, ext, panel_id, day)`. RLS on | `SELECT` for `authenticated` when `relay_private.is_owner()`; no other grants or policies |
| `relay_private.config` | one row: `owner_id uuid`, `status_token_sha256 bytea` (32 bytes). PER-304 moved `ingest_token_sha256` to `ingest_tokens` | `postgres` only (not in an exposed schema) |
| `relay_private.ingest_tokens` | PER-304: `instance text primary key` (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$`), `token_sha256 bytea unique` (32 bytes), `created_at`. No hash may equal the status token's (a statement trigger on both tables, `23514`) | `postgres` only |
| `relay_private.ingest_instance(p_token, p_instance) -> text` | PER-304: the token's instance; `PT403` for an unknown token, or when `p_instance` is given and differs | the write RPCs only |
| `relay_private.is_owner() -> boolean` | `auth.uid() = owner_id and auth.jwt()->>'aal' = 'aal2'` | used by the RLS policies |
| `public.relay_ingest(p_token text, p_data jsonb, p_instance text default null) -> void` | checks, in order: the token's instance from `ingest_tokens` (403; also 403 when `p_instance` names another); an object (400); a `data.instance` object whose `name` is not the token's instance (403); `octet_length(data::text)` ≤ 256 KiB (413; a schema-1 push ≤ 32 KiB); `tier = 'remote'` and `schema` 1 or 2 (400; schema 1 also still refuses the old local-only fields: `trust_rules`, `repos`, `claude_brain`, the `items` of `runs`, `approvals`, `questions` and `pulse`, `runner.missing`, `health[].reason`); no `remote_url` key at any depth and no non-null `approvals.items[*].code` or `approvals.recent[*].code` (400); no secret-shaped text, `p_data::text ~ redact.SECRET_SQL_FAMILY` (400, `relay: secret-shaped text`); a `taken_at` no more than 5 min in the future (400); at most 3 rows per minute per instance (429). Then deletes history rows received more than 7 days ago (every instance's), inserts the instance's history row (slim for schema 2), upserts its `snapshot_latest` row (only when its `taken_at` is not newer than the new one: a late arrival never replaces a newer snapshot) and keeps at most the newest 12000 history rows of that instance. An old daemon's two-argument call still resolves (`p_instance` null) and lands in `resonant` | `anon` (with the ingest token) |
| `public.relay_panel_day(p_token text, p_ext text, p_panel text, p_day text, p_data jsonb, p_keep_days int, p_instance text default null) -> void` | PER-286, the pusher's day upload. Checks, in order: the token's instance, as `relay_ingest` (403); ext and panel slugs (400); a `YYYY-MM-DD` day no later than tomorrow and at most 731 days back (400); `p_keep_days` 7-730 (400); `p_data` null (delete that day: a rebuild found no data) or an object ≤ 64 KiB (413) with no `local_url` or `remote_url` key at any depth, no secret-shaped text (`redact.SECRET_SQL_FAMILY`) and no Remote Control link (400); at most 30 rows received per minute per instance (429). Then upserts the instance's row, deletes that instance's panel rows older than `current_date - p_keep_days`, and keeps at most 20000 rows an instance (the oldest days go) | `anon` (with the ingest token) |
| `public.relay_status(token text) -> jsonb` | DB-4's contract (`relay/README.md`): `POST /rest/v1/rpc/relay_status` `{"token": ...}`. A wrong token (or none configured) raises `42501`, never null; no snapshots at all: `null`; else (PER-304) `{"instances": [{"instance", "taken_at", "health": [{"name", "state"}], "secrets": [{"ref", "expires_at", "days_left"}]}, ...], "taken_at": ..., "health": [...]}`: one entry per instance with an ingest token, by name, from its newest `snapshots` row by `taken_at` (`taken_at` null, `health` and `secrets` `[]` for one that never pushed; `secrets` since PER-315, `[]` for an old daemon's row), plus the old top-level fields from `resonant`'s newest row (`null` and `[]` when it has none), so a checker from before PER-304 is unchanged. Nothing more (a slim row has both). `volatile`, so PostgREST takes POST only and the token never sits in a URL | `anon` (with the status token, `RELAY_STATUS_TOKEN` on Fly): the dead-man checker |

`relay_ingest` errors use PostgREST's `PTxxx` codes, so the HTTP status is the reason: 403 bad token, 400 not a remote-tier snapshot, a link or code, secret-shaped text or a bad `taken_at`, 413 too large, 429 rate limited. Both tokens are bearer secrets: a holder of the ingest token can push fake snapshots, so it lives only in the Keychain. The dashboard reads `snapshot_latest` as the owner (see Read path above). The anon key alone can't select, insert, update or delete anything, or read the config. `tests/test_supabase_sql.py` runs the migrations on a real Postgres with a stub of Supabase's roles, `auth.uid()`/`auth.jwt()` and default privileges (`tests/fixtures/supabase_stub.sql`); the `supabase-sql` CI job runs it (it skips locally without Postgres binaries: set `PG_TEST_BINDIR`). It checks the full snapshot is stored as latest and slim in the history, a schema-1 push is stored whole, an older `taken_at` doesn't overwrite the latest, each secret family and a link or approval code are refused with nothing stored, the 256 KiB cap, that only the aal2 owner reads `snapshot_latest`, and that `relay_status` still returns health after a slim row. PER-304 adds: a Cori token writes only the `cori` rows, a mismatched `p_instance` or snapshot instance gets 403, a push without `p_instance` lands in `resonant`, the rate limits and row caps are per instance, `panel_days` per instance, the `relay_status` shape (new and old fields), and the migration applied to the schema before it keeps the existing rows and token as `resonant`. PER-315 adds: the slim row and `relay_status` keep the first 5 expiries (`{ref, expires_at, days_left}` only; ref names such as `relay/supabase_key` pass the secret check), an old daemon's push, a schema-1 row and a non-list `secrets` read as `[]`, and the migration over PER-304's schema keeps the old checker's fields. `tests/test_redact_clip.py` checks the migration holds `SECRET_SQL_FAMILY` exactly and that `redact_secrets` replaces everything it matches. The stub proves privileges, not PostgREST's HTTP mapping of the `PTxxx` codes.

### Fly relay (`relay/`, DB-4)

A Python standard-library app on one Fly machine ([`relay/README.md`](../relay/README.md) has the settings and deploy steps; go-live step 15 is the owner walkthrough). It never connects to the Mini.

- **Serves** the committed `dashboard/dist` with a `config.json` generated at boot, `{"source": "supabase", "url": SUPABASE_URL, "anon_key": SUPABASE_ANON_KEY}`; the browser signs in (password + TOTP, `aal2`) and reads `snapshot_latest` itself (falling back to the newest `snapshots` row until a daemon has pushed schema 2). `GET /healthz` is `{"ok": true}` (503 when the checker has stalled) and says nothing about Resonant.
- **Checks** every `CHECK_EVERY` (60 s) through `relay_status(RELAY_STATUS_TOKEN)` and keeps three incidents, each alerted once when it opens and once on recovery: `stale` (newest `taken_at` older than `STALE_AFTER`, default 5 min, or no rows), `failing` (a health row in state `failing`) and `read_error` (reads failing for `READ_FAIL_AFTER`). Alerts go to ntfy (`NTFY_TOPIC`, optional `NTFY_URL`, `NTFY_TOKEN`) and/or `WEBHOOK_URL` (JSON `{source, kind, event, title, message, at}`, plus `instance` on a non-`resonant` stale/failing alert). State is in memory, so exactly one machine.
- **Per instance** (PER-305). `RELAY_INSTANCES` (`[env]`, default `resonant`) lists the watched instances; `stale` and `failing` are kept per `(instance, kind)` from `relay_status`'s `instances[]` (an answer without it parses as `resonant`'s; `RELAY_READ=table` reads one `snapshots` row per instance, filtered by `instance`). A listed instance that never pushed is stale; an unlisted one's rows are ignored. `read_error` stays global and goes to the global channels only. Alerts carry the display name (`name.capitalize()`); `resonant`'s text is unchanged. `RELAY_NOTIFY_<INSTANCE>` (`ntfy`, `webhook`, `slack`) picks an instance's channels; the default is the global channels plus `slack` when `SLACK_WEBHOOK_<INSTANCE>` is set. `slack` is `SlackWebhookNotifier` (`slack:<instance>`): `POST {"text": "🔴 Cori: …" | "✅ Cori: …"}` to that instance's Incoming Webhook. A Slack URL that isn't `https://hooks.slack.com/…`, or `slack` listed without one, is a `ConfigError` at start; no URL and no explicit list is just no Slack. Retries are per channel, so a failed Slack post never repeats ntfy.
- **Credential expiries** (PER-315). `source.parse_statuses` reads each `instances[]` entry's `secrets` into `Status.secrets` (`Expiry(ref, expires_at, days_left)`, at most 5) when present; an answer without it (before the migration), a non-list, or a malformed entry is none, never a `ReadError`. No new alert kind: the daemon's `core/secrets` job alerts, and an expired secret already shows as a `failing` `secrets` health row, which the `failing` incident covers. The page shows a **Credentials** card under System status (`dashboard/src/sections/SystemStatus.tsx`, `lib/credentials.ts`): each instance's soonest 3 from its newest snapshot (the shown one's own, the others read every 60 s by `usePeerSnapshots` through the same loaders as the switcher), side by side when there is more than one, `ref 300d` or `364d (assumed)`, red when expired (`days_left < 0` or `expires_at <= taken_at`) or `probe: rejected`, amber within 7 days, plain otherwise, `as of` its `taken_at`. An instance whose snapshot has no `secrets` shows nothing; none anywhere: no card. Same card on the local page.
- **Secrets** (`fly secrets`): `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (an `sb_publishable_` key or an anon-role JWT, refused otherwise), `RELAY_STATUS_TOKEN` (its SHA-256 is `relay_private.config.status_token_sha256`), `NTFY_TOPIC` and the optional alert settings (`NTFY_TOKEN`, `WEBHOOK_URL`, `SLACK_WEBHOOK_<INSTANCE>`, all redacted from logs). Nothing with write access lives on Fly.
- **Deploy** (PER-316): `resonant relay deploy [--dry-run] [--repo PATH]` (`resonant/ops/relay_deploy.py`, CLI group `relay`). Runs `[fly, "deploy", "--app", relay.fly_app, "--config", "relay/fly.toml", "--dockerfile", "relay/Dockerfile", "--ha=false", "."]` with `cwd` = `--repo` or `ops.repo_path`, then `fly status --app <app> --json` and prints the machine count (a warning when it isn't 1). The child's env is `ops_env()` plus `fly`'s dir first on `PATH` plus `FLY_API_TOKEN` = the Keychain item `relay/fly_deploy_token` (`FLY_DEPLOY_TOKEN_REF`, personal Resonant's service); an inherited `FLY_API_TOKEN` or `FLY_ACCESS_TOKEN` never passes. The token is never in argv, output (fly's streamed lines are masked), an error or the audit. `fly` is `relay.fly_path`, else `fly`/`flyctl` on the caller's `PATH`, else `/opt/homebrew/bin/fly`. Refusals, before any subprocess or token read: a non-default instance (exit 2: the relay is shared and the token is personal's), no `relay.fly_app`, no `fly`, no repo (or no `relay/fly.toml`/`relay/Dockerfile`), no `dashboard/dist` (exit 1); a missing token is exit 1 with the setup lines (`fly tokens create deploy -a <app> --expiry 8760h`, `resonant secret set relay/fly_deploy_token --expires-in 365d`), an unreadable Keychain 75. Exit code: fly's own (124 after the 30 min timeout); a non-zero exit with a known auth phrase adds one line, `Fly rejected the token: renew with …`. `--dry-run` prints the argv and `FLY_API_TOKEN=<from relay/fly_deploy_token>`, asking the Keychain only whether the item exists (no `-w`). Audit `relay.deploy` `{app, exit_code, head}` (`requested_by` `owner:cli`, `head` the repo's `git rev-parse HEAD` or null); not on a dry run or a refusal. With `relay.fly_app` set on personal Resonant, PER-313's `secret_expiry.known_refs` includes `relay/fly_deploy_token` (`resonant secret list`, the daily `core/secrets` watch), renew hint `fly tokens create deploy -a <relay.fly_app> --expiry 8760h`.
